CRM · Investor Day Notes · 2026-09-16 · 白底版 (截图用)

Salesforce 2026 Investor Day (2026-09-16，San Francisco，Dreamforce 期间) 初读笔记

逐段英文原文、中文翻译和解读，外加关键数字、术语表和之后要盯的事。给第一次读这家公司投资者日的人。

这场会讲了什么

先搞懂这家公司

这不是财报电话会，而是 CRM 在年度客户大会 Dreamforce 期间开的投资者日，约 3 小时，结构和往年不同：前半场几乎全是现场演示 (总裁 Patrick Stokes 演示 AIforce 和 Claudeforce，Siemens CIO 上台，数据负责人 Rohan Kumar 讲 Data 360 和 Informatica)，然后是 go-to-market (Miguel Milano、Alexa Vignone)、两场客户和伙伴对谈 (Adecco、Anthropic)、CFO Robin Washington 讲财务框架，最后是 Benioff 一个人回答 8 个分析师问题。

CRM 主要按人头 (每个销售、客服用户每月多少钱) 收订阅费，AI 通过升级到更贵的版本 (Core $195、Advanced $395、Max $550 每用户每月)、Flex Credits 用量和 AELA (全公司一口价包年) 来收。这场会要回答的问题和我们 CRM - Deep 一样：AI 让销售和客服的人变少以后，每个客户的账单靠什么继续涨。

读之前先知道：这场会没有新的财务目标，FY30 $63B 框架维持 (增量全是 Informatica)，有机再加速仍只有定性说法。最有信息量的是三件事：管理层说席位“没下降”但不给数字；Claudeforce 让用户不登录 Salesforce 也能用，但目前免费；最大的 AI 交易多是 AELA 包年，用量涨了不多收钱。演示段落可以跳读，必读集中在 go-to-market、CFO 和问答。

这场会

一句话：没有新目标：FY30 $63B 维持 (去年从 $60B 上调的部分全是 Informatica)，有机再加速仍不给数字；AI 变现的真实路径是“推动升级” (Max 版 $550、每 1% 用户升级 = $1 亿 AOV，但账户 ARR 只涨 1.4 倍) 加 AELA 包年，Claudeforce 和每月 15 亿次 Anthropic 调用目前不收钱；Benioff 对“什么替代席位”完全回避。

  • FY30 收入框架维持 $63B 不变 (去年 $60B 加 Informatica $3B)，CFO 重申 H2 FY27 起有机收入再加速，但仍没有给有机增速数字，也没有新的 FY30 利润率或 FCF 目标。
  • 变现重心是版本升级：十多年来首个新的价值版本，基数每升级 1% = 1 亿美元，8 个季度累计 10 亿美元 AOV；但标价提升 60% 到 80%，客户层面 ARR 只扩大到 1.4 倍，因为客户在升级时砍掉了其他产品。
  • 管理层称销售和服务席位“没有任何下降、仍在增长”，但没给数字，并提前铺垫“即便将来下降也有新商业框架弥补”；同场的 CRWD 案例 (服务 agent 自主处理 83% 进线请求) 恰恰是席位风险的例子。
  • Claudeforce 目前免费公测 (约 40 家试点客户)，GA 后要付费，收费路径是打包进每用户每月 550 美元的 AIforce Max 版；旗舰客户 Siemens (SIE) 的 CIO 在台上说定价模式还没谈好，试点 25 人，潜在 18,000 人。
  • Claudeforce 的使用量和收入脱钩：Anthropic 一家每月在 Salesforce 里执行约 15 亿次操作，主持人当众开玩笑说“应该按你们收入的百分比收费”。Benioff 说 token 转售和智能路由会变现，但“不是第一步”。
  • 大客户越来越多用 AELA (全公司一口价包年) 买 AI：Q2 前 10 大交易有 7 笔是 AELA；新的 Salesforce Commit 被称为收入加速的主要驱动力，但没给数字。
  • Adecco 样板案例说明 AI 收入怎么来：先用 Data Cloud 止住流失，再签两年期 AELA 包年合同 (无限额度) 一次性抬高支出；之后用量再涨也不多收钱，要等续约，Salesforce 想改成按客户业务结果分成。
  • 现金回报是这一段最实在的数字：FY27 FCF 约 $15B；史上最大 ASR 10 月完成，预期均价 $182/股、股本减少 14% 以上；累计回购 $60B，股息 $3B。Benioff 还透露 ASR 有借债，打算卖出价值可能达数百亿美元的 Anthropic 股权来还债。
  • 管理层承认的战略重点是“推动升级”：Coworker 等新界面只对 Max 版或 A1E 开放，增长靠同一坐席卖更贵的版本 (增长类型 B)。对“什么替代坐席”和“再加速能否平稳”，Benioff 完全回避。
  • CFO 给的客户扩张数字都是精选样本：AWU 前 100 客户 18 个月 ARR 约 2 倍，某公共部门客户两年 ARR 约 4 倍，AI 原生公司坐席 +62%，长期“ARR 3 到 4 倍机会”没有时间表。

pipeline 增速very high-teens (原文未给精确数)Miguel 称为新信息，未给精确数；创纪录，覆盖率好于往年
每季度交易笔数约 100,000 笔每个金额档位的合同期限都在变长，会推高 RPO/cRPO
AI 公司客户业务约 6 倍 (原文同时说 400%)约 +400% 到 +500%前 10 大 AI 公司中 9 家全面使用 Salesforce，10 家都用 Slack；没给绝对金额

Slack 数据管道新业务约 3 笔已成交过去 90 天出现，按 API 访问而非人头收费，规模很小

AE 产能年底核心国家 AE 同比 +10% 到 12%，builder 1,000 人新的量化口径对利润率框架的影响留待 Robin Washington 部分

怎么读这份笔记

这场投资者日和电话会不同：前半场是产品演示，管理层讲得更长、更宣传，数字少；演示部分可以跳读，必读集中在 go-to-market、CFO 环节和问答。电话会通常分三段：主持人和投资者关系负责人的开场套话、管理层的准备发言 (CEO 讲业务和产品，CFO 讲数字和指引)、分析师问答。准备发言是写好稿子念的，偏宣传；问答才是信息量最大的部分，因为分析师会追问管理层不想主动讲的东西。

这份笔记把整场投资者日切成 173 段，每段左边是英文原文、右边是中文翻译，下面是我们的解读。每段标了重要程度：必读 39 段，有用，可跳过 (套话，默认折叠)。原文里最关键的一句用底色标出。时间紧就只读"必读"。

管理层发言

Mark Murphy 全球投资者关系 EVP 可跳过 套话：主持人请全球投资者关系 EVP Mark Murphy 上台。他欢迎大家，感谢到场，鼓励投资人在 Dreamfor
Please welcome to the stage EVP, Global Investor Relations, Mark Murphy. Thank you. Welcome, everyone. Great to be here. Look at the energy in this room. Love it. Love it. As mentioned, I am Mark Murphy, EVP of Global Investor Relations with Salesforce. We have just a fantastic Investor Day lined up for you. I want to start by thanking everyone in this room. We are so grateful to you, for your time and attention. We hope you're going to make the most of this unbelievable event by getting out there and engaging with customers and partners. Ask them anything you want. Ask them what's changing. Ask them how this transformation is occurring for them. This is at least my 20th Dreamforce, if you can believe that, I've kind of lost count, but it's at least 20. And it's just unbelievable to be here and to be on this side of the room for the first time. It means so much to me. And as I reflect back on having been here through 20 Dreamforces, what is actually amazing is what has not changed at all. The location hasn't changed. We're still here in San Francisco at Moscone Center, the keynotes in the same room. The founders haven't changed. We have Marc, we have Parker sitting right over here. They're going to be on stage later today. The energy level hasn't changed. At the beginning of the keynote, the Hawaiian ceremony hasn't changed. I think a lot of the jokes that get made have not changed somehow in 20 years.
套话：主持人请全球投资者关系 EVP Mark Murphy 上台。他欢迎大家，感谢到场，鼓励投资人在 Dreamforce 现场多和客户、合作伙伴聊，问他们发生了什么变化、转型进行得怎样。他说这至少是他第 20 次参加 Dreamforce，这是第一次站在 IR 这一侧。20 年来地点 (旧金山 Moscone Center)、创始人 (Marc Benioff 和 Parker Harris，今天稍后会上台)、现场气氛、夏威夷开场仪式乃至很多笑话都没变。可跳过。
Mark Murphy 全球投资者关系 EVP 有用
What has changed is the technology. And the technology has changed quite a bit over the summer. There's a new ability to unlock trapped value. That has legged up in a pretty exponential way in the last couple of months. And so, because of that, we're going to have a totally different structure to the Analyst Day. It's going to be unlike other Analyst Days you've seen, we are going to be opening up laptops and we're just going to be showing you live, this kind of transformation that is happening. If we're going to be trying to show you what's possible, we're also going to have many more customers on stage. We're going to have Siemens. We're going to have Adecco, and we're actually going to have Anthropic on stage toward the end of this event.
变的是技术。技术在这个夏天变化很大，出现了一种释放“被困住的价值”(trapped value) 的新能力，过去几个月呈指数式加速。因此今天的分析师日结构完全不同：我们会打开笔记本电脑，现场演示正在发生的转型；也会请更多客户上台，包括 Siemens、Adecco，活动快结束时 Anthropic 也会上台。
解读定调：今天不是传统的“PPT + 财务模型”分析师日，而是以现场演示为主，重点是和 Anthropic 合作的 Claudeforce。把客户 Siemens (SIE)、Adecco (ADEN) 和 Anthropic 都请上台，是想用第三方背书回应“AI 杀死 SaaS”的担忧。读的时候要分清哪些是演示效果、哪些是有数字的经营事实。

One more thing that has not changed in the last 20 years is the legal disclaimer. And so, I'm not going to read it to you, but we're going to make some forward-looking statements that are subject to change. And we would strongly encourage you to refer to our most recent SEC filings, including 10-Qs and 10-Ks. So, this agenda is going to be fast and efficient. We're going to really fly through this. We're going to start with Patrick and Rohan. They're going to go a lot deeper on the technology. We're then going to move to the go-to-market execution engine. We're going to have Miguel and Alexa for that section. And then batting cleanup, we're going to have Robin with the financial framework for this agentic opportunity. After that, Marc is going to join us, and Marc will be on stage and we will have a Q&A with the full leadership team for as long as they want to go at the end and after that, we're going to have an investor reception and demos. That's actually going to be right outside this room.
套话：20 年没变的还有法律免责声明，不念了，提醒会有前瞻性陈述，请参考最新的 10-Q 和 10-K。议程：先由 Patrick 和 Rohan 深入讲技术；然后是 go-to-market 执行引擎，由 Miguel 和 Alexa 讲；最后由 Robin 讲这个 agentic 机会的财务框架；之后 Marc 上台，全体管理层问答；最后在会场外有投资者酒会和演示。下面交给应用与营销总裁 Patrick。
解读议程顺序本身有信息：财务框架 (Robin Washington) 和问答都在本段之后，所以本段 (第一部分) 没有 FY30 框架数字，也没有 Agentforce ARR 数字。
Patrick Stokes 应用与营销总裁 (President, Applications and Marketing) 可跳过 套话：Mark 把话筒交给应用与营销总裁 Patrick。Patrick 开玩笑说议程不会像 Mark 说的那么“快速高
So, with that, I'm going to hand it over to Patrick, who is President of Applications and Marketing. Patrick, welcome. All right. Thanks, everybody. Does anybody want to take any bets on whether this agenda is fast and efficient, as Mark put it? Because I'll be happy to take a couple bets. I think I know where it will get slow, but that's okay. Okay. So, I'm psyched to be here with you all today. Don't look at me like that, Miguel. And we're going to walk you through a whole bunch of stuff about where we're going. We're going to kind of break down what you saw in the keynote a little bit more today. I've been getting a lot of questions from yesterday on how did we do that. It's funny, there's like two sides of belief. There's the side that kind of has an inkling and kind of wants to know more detail. And then there's the other side that's like that couldn't have been real and it totally was. So, we're going to break that down and show you how all of that works.
套话：Mark 把话筒交给应用与营销总裁 Patrick。Patrick 开玩笑说议程不会像 Mark 说的那么“快速高效”。他说昨天很多人问主题演讲里的演示是怎么做到的：一类人隐约有感觉想了解细节，另一类人觉得那不可能是真的，但它确实是真的，今天就来拆解。
Patrick Stokes 应用与营销总裁 (President, Applications and Marketing) 有用
Rohan and I, the way to kind of think about these two sections in the agenda here is, I'm going to start by walking you through how I think our apps are going to change and how they need to become agent ready or a more technical term, agent legible, meaning every app needs to be usable by agents in a composable environment like Cowork, like you saw earlier. And then Rohan is going to come up and start walking you through how we are in the midst of transforming into a much bigger data company and how we'll leverage that data in an enterprise harness for the future. So, that's the course of the next 30 minutes or 40 minutes or so. Now, what I want to do is something just mildly annoying for a moment is, I want to hop out of the slides and I want to go to the demo screen just for a second, if we could. Thank you, team back there. Okay. So, I mentioned – well, actually, how many of you saw the keynote yesterday? Oh, most of you. Okay, great. I thought it'd be about a third of you. Good job, everybody.
我和 Rohan 这两部分可以这样理解：我先讲我们的应用会怎么变，它们需要变成“agent 就绪”，更技术一点的说法是“agent 可读”(agent legible)，意思是每个应用都要能在 Cowork 这样的可组合环境里被 agent 使用。然后 Rohan 会讲我们正在转型为一家大得多的数据公司，以及我们如何把这些数据用在面向未来的企业 harness (企业 AI 底座) 里。接下来大概 30 到 40 分钟。现在我要做一件稍微烦人的事：先跳出幻灯片，切到演示屏幕。
解读关键词“agent legible”：以前软件是给人点的，以后要能被 AI agent (这里主要是 Anthropic 的 Claude Cowork) 直接调用。这等于承认用户入口可能从 Salesforce 自己的界面转移到第三方 AI 工具。对投资的含义：如果入口不再是 Salesforce 界面，按“登录用户数”收费的逻辑就要改，后面要看他们怎么收钱。
Patrick Stokes 应用与营销总裁 (President, Applications and Marketing) 可跳过 演示开场：我在主题演讲里说过，从零到一个相当可用的东西大约需要 6 到 8 分钟，之后可能还要几天迭代界面。我这里写了一
So, I mentioned in the keynote yesterday that you can go from nothing to something pretty usable in about six to eight minutes. And then after that, you might have a few days of iteration as you build and you refine and you kind of design the user interface that you want. So, I want to try to bring that to life a little bit here. So, I've got a prompt up here and I worded it kind of in a funny way, but I'm saying build a one-shot artifact. An artifact is actually a capability within Cowork, which you'll see in a moment, and I wanted to list my open Salesforce opportunities. I wanted to use the Headless Toolkit to do it. And then I wanted to build a model where Claude drafts a task and posts that over to Slack. And then I say no questions. One shot at the front and no questions at the back. Or me trying to get Claude not to ask me any damn questions and just build the thing, so that I don't have to keep coming back in and hit enter while this is going. And what I'm going to do is this is like the cooking show. If you've ever seen a cooking show where they pull the turkey out of the oven, even though it's been in there all day, is we're going to hit enter and you're going to see this start and it's going to start working on this application, this opportunity application that I just asked. It's loading the tools. And all in all, this is probably going to take about six minutes end to end to come back with a completely working application.
演示开场：我在主题演讲里说过，从零到一个相当可用的东西大约需要 6 到 8 分钟，之后可能还要几天迭代界面。我这里写了一个提示词：做一个“一次成型”的 artifact (Cowork 里的一种功能)，列出我在 Salesforce 里的未结商机，用 Headless Toolkit 来做，再让 Claude 起草一个任务并发到 Slack；最后写“不要提问”，就是想让 Claude 别老问我问题，直接做完。这就像烹饪节目里把烤了一整天的火鸡从烤箱里拿出来：按下回车，它会开始工作、加载工具，端到端大约 6 分钟会生成一个完整可用的应用。
解读演示：用自然语言让 Claude 读取 Salesforce 数据并生成小应用。没有任何收费或数字信息，信息量低。值得记住的只有一点：调用 Salesforce 走的是 Headless Toolkit (也就是 MCP 接口)，而不是人登录 Salesforce 界面。

Okay. So, I think you heard yesterday that we really believe that there is a big interface revolution going on. Another way that I like to say this is that there is a user behavior or a user experience revolution going on, which is that most people in the world, in software at least, are starting to figure out or starting to sense that there is a better way to use software, right? The way that we've used software for 50 years is we have to know what we want to do and we have to know then how to use the software itself. And we have to click around and hunt and peck.
大家昨天听到了，我们确实认为一场巨大的界面革命正在发生。换个说法，是用户行为或用户体验的革命：大多数人 (至少在软件领域) 开始意识到有一种更好的使用软件的方式。过去 50 年，我们得先知道自己要做什么，再知道怎么用这个软件，然后到处点来点去地找。
解读管理层主动把“界面革命”当主题，这是在抢先定义叙事：与其被说成“AI 绕过 SaaS 界面”，不如自己说“我们拥抱它”。问题是界面被绕过之后，靠什么维持单客户账单，这是我们 CRM - Deep 的核心问题。
Patrick Stokes 应用与营销总裁 (President, Applications and Marketing) 有用
The analogy that I like to use is that if you're a digital photographer, like a wedding photographer, part of your job is being having a good eye and knowing how to use your camera. But like most of your job is just knowing how to use Photoshop, right? Like it's a big, complicated application. Salesforce is the same way. It's a big application. It has a ton of surface area and your ability to extract value out of it is limited to the humans' ability of what they know about how to use the application. And what's happening now is we are taking an agent and putting it between the software and the human and we're just taking intent from the human. It's giving it to the agent, to the knowledge worker agent. In this case, what you'll see today, that knowledge worker agent is Claude. Claude is looking across all of the tools and capabilities, which have been connected inside of Cowork and it is composing a response based on all of those tools. So, you are getting a tremendous amount of additional value out of not just Salesforce, but any other platforms that you plug into it.
我喜欢用一个类比：如果你是数码摄影师，比如婚礼摄影师，工作的一部分是眼光好、会用相机，但大部分工作其实是会用 Photoshop，那是个又大又复杂的应用。Salesforce 也一样，是个大应用，功能面非常广，你能从中获得多少价值，受限于人对这个应用的了解程度。现在发生的是，我们在软件和人之间放一个 agent，只从人那里拿“意图”，交给知识工作者 agent。今天你们看到的这个知识工作者 agent 就是 Claude。Claude 会查看 Cowork 里连接的所有工具和能力，基于这些工具组织答案。所以你不只是从 Salesforce，还能从你接入的任何其他平台获得大量额外价值。
解读这段话的潜台词很重要：过去 Salesforce 的价值有一部分“锁在”复杂界面和培训成本里 (就像 Photoshop 的熟练度)，现在 Claude 把这层复杂度抹平了。好处是更多人能用、用得更深；坏处是 Salesforce 变成 Claude 背后的一个数据和工具源，用户界面和品牌存在感下降。目前没有给出任何调用量或收费数字。
Patrick Stokes 应用与营销总裁 (President, Applications and Marketing) 有用
The other interesting thing that's happening there is it's kind of aggregating software into one environment because no longer do I have to do a discrete task in Salesforce and then a discrete task in SAP, and a discrete task in Workday. If I have a task that requires all three of those systems, if they're all wired up in the same way that we just showed you yesterday, like we can do with Salesforce, if they're all in that Cowork environment, it's now all aggregated and that agent can go off and kind of work and operate across those systems in ways that, frankly, we've been trying to build integrations for, for the last 50 years. And now we have this entirely new way to kind of drive these integrations. So, it's a big, big revolution.
另一个有意思的变化是，它在把软件聚合到一个环境里。我不再需要在 Salesforce 里做一件事、在 SAP 里做一件事、在 Workday 里做一件事。如果一个任务需要这三个系统，而它们都像我们昨天展示的 Salesforce 那样接好了、都在 Cowork 环境里，那就被聚合了，agent 可以跨系统操作，坦白说这正是过去 50 年我们一直想用集成去做的事。现在有了驱动这些集成的全新方式。所以这是一场非常大的革命。
解读“聚合”是一个双刃剑的说法。在 AI 时代，谁占据“聚合层”谁就有议价权，这里聚合层是 Anthropic 的 Cowork，而 Salesforce、SAP、WDAY 都变成被聚合的供应方。用生活例子说：就像外卖平台聚合了餐馆，餐馆还在赚钱，但流量入口在平台手里。管理层讲得很兴奋，但没讲 Salesforce 在这个结构里怎么保住定价权。
Patrick Stokes 应用与营销总裁 (President, Applications and Marketing) 有用
And of course, we've been working towards this. Marc talked through this yesterday, really trying to simplify our platform to make it ready for this user behavior change that we see happening. When we click into Claudeforce just for a moment and I'll show you this in the app, basically, we came off of Headless at our developer conference back in March. We launched Headless. We kind of we sensed the user behavior change that was happening and we were like, we better get MCP servers out because our customers are trying to do this themself and it's a little bit dangerous. So, let's get some first-party MCP servers out. That was, I think, very successful and a little surprising. I don't think people expected that posture from a software company to actually endorse that pattern. We got out there with it quickly and then we started seeing people pick up and use it very quickly. And then on top of that, we started seeing them run into problems that we needed to fix. Patrick Stokes 应用与营销总裁 (President, Applications and Marketing) 可跳过 演示：典型的 Salesforce 问题是什么样？比如“告诉我我的未结 pipeline”。后台发生的是：先从问题里推断
And so, what would a typical Salesforce question look like? Well, it might be, tell me about my pipeline, let's say open pipeline. So, a fairly typical question. Now, what's going to go on in the background here as this thinks and you'll see the work going on here is first that it looks at the question and it's deriving the intent from the question. So, just from the word pipeline, it is looking then at its tools that have been connected and it is trying to connect a tool to the intent. Okay. And in this case, it's going to connect Salesforce to that intent. It's going to say this is a question that Salesforce can likely answer, and then it's going to go ask Salesforce that question through the MCP server, and then it's going to bring back and answer. That is what is happening behind the screens.
演示：典型的 Salesforce 问题是什么样？比如“告诉我我的未结 pipeline”。后台发生的是：先从问题里推断意图，仅凭 pipeline 这个词，它就去查已连接的工具，把某个工具和这个意图对上。这里它会对上 Salesforce，判断这是 Salesforce 能回答的问题，然后通过 MCP 服务器去问 Salesforce，再把答案带回来。这就是幕后发生的事。
解读这里把“MCP 调用”讲得很直白：Claude 每回答一次 Salesforce 问题，就调用一次 Salesforce 的 MCP 服务器。这类调用就是我们说的“Headless 调用”，如果将来按调用量收费，这是用量的计量点；目前没有提收费。
Patrick Stokes 应用与营销总裁 (President, Applications and Marketing) 可跳过 演示：它不仅带回了答案，还以一个动态生成的迷你应用的形式出现在对话里，这得益于 MCP 里一个叫 MCP apps 的新
And not only did it bring back an answer, but you'll notice that it brought it back as a dynamically generated kind of mini application right here in the channel, and that is all made possible through a new protocol within MCP called MCP apps. Sorry to get mildly technical for a second, you'll also hear us refer to it as HXL, which is just our internal name for it. And all it means is when we ask that question, Salesforce on the other side, it says, not only do I know the answer to that question, but let me give it back to you with some additional detail about what an application could look like on the other side. And then Claude says, thank you very much for that information, and it draws the application right here in the chat and all of that took place, everything that I just said took place in, well, way faster than I was even able to tell you what was going on, right?
演示：它不仅带回了答案，还以一个动态生成的迷你应用的形式出现在对话里，这得益于 MCP 里一个叫 MCP apps 的新协议 (内部叫 HXL)。意思是我们提问时，Salesforce 那边说：我不仅知道答案，还给你附上一个应用可以长什么样的细节。然后 Claude 说谢谢你的信息，就在对话里把这个应用画出来。我刚才说的这一切，发生得比我讲解的速度还快。
解读MCP apps 的意义：Salesforce 试图在别人的界面 (Claude) 里保留自己的“界面控制权”，即数据用什么样式展示由 Salesforce 决定。这是被聚合时保住存在感的技术手段。

Patrick Stokes 应用与营销总裁 (President, Applications and Marketing) 有用
Now, these seem like relatively simple queries and they kind of are. But I want you to think through how much kind of internal human clicking is going on inside of Salesforce to do this the old way, right? You have to know how to get to opportunities. You have to load all of your opportunities. You have to click into every single one. You have to look at the entire opportunity screen for every single opportunity. You have to commit to memory, what's going on with those opportunities. Then you have to synthesize that as a human being, and then that tells you what you should go work on, or you can just ask Claude. And that's effectively what we've done here with Claudeforce.
这些看起来是相对简单的查询，确实也是。但想想按老办法在 Salesforce 里人要点多少下：要知道怎么进入商机页面，加载所有商机，逐个点进去，看每个商机的完整页面，记住每个商机的情况，然后作为人去综合判断，才知道该去做什么。或者你直接问 Claude 就行。这就是我们用 Claudeforce 做到的。
解读这段是“界面革命”对 Salesforce 自身商业模式最直接的含义：过去用户在 Salesforce 界面里花的时间 (点击、查找) 是产品“黏性”的一部分，现在被一句提问替代。用户价值上升，但用户在 Salesforce 界面里的时间下降。如果收费仍按人头，单人价值上升可以支撑涨价 (Max 版)；如果企业因此减少需要的销售人数，人头就会减少。
Patrick Stokes 应用与营销总裁 (President, Applications and Marketing) 有用
Now, let me show you a couple other things. One thing that's very cool is if there are certain things that you do every single day or every few hours or whatever it is, you can schedule these. So, that's what I have. I have a daily briefing skill here. So, here's the daily briefings. So, this tells me what I should be worried about every single morning. And I don't even have to come in and run this every single morning. I can just tell Claude to schedule it. It saves it as a daily briefing schedule, which you see right up here. And in a funny way, what you've done there is you've basically created just by asking, you've created an agent, you have created an agent that every morning is going to go out, it's going to inspect Salesforce. It's going to build your daily briefing, and it's going to bring it back and drop it right here in Claude. And, of course, that goes to your phone as well. You get a notification every morning. So, it's an incredibly powerful capability.
演示：再展示几样东西。很酷的一点是，如果有些事你每天或每隔几小时都要做，可以设成定时任务。我这里有个“每日简报”技能 (skill)，告诉我每天早上该担心什么。我不用每天早上手动运行，只要让 Claude 定时执行，它会保存为每日简报的计划任务。说起来有点好笑，你只是开口要求，就等于创建了一个 agent：它每天早上去检查 Salesforce，生成你的每日简报，放回 Claude 里，手机上也会收到通知。这是非常强大的能力。
解读“提一个要求就等于建了一个 agent”。这意味着 agent 的数量会暴增，而且是在 Claude 里建的，不一定在 Agentforce 里。这对 Salesforce 的收费口径是个问题：这些 agent 每天调用 Salesforce 数据，但是否计入 Agentforce 或任何用量收费，没说。

And then finally, what makes this all work? Well, it's pretty straightforward. 最后，是什么让这一切运转？很简单，就是幕后这个新插件 (plug-in)：我们把让 Salesforce 运转的众多 MCP 服务器和一些技能打包在一起。目前大多数技能面向销售问题和销售人员，但会扩展到整个平台。然后因为人和软件之间有了一个强大的 agent，事情就自然运转起来。关键在于，这不只是回答问题，而是构建你开展业务所需的完整应用和仪表盘。实际上今天早上发生了一件事，我本来不打算提，但还是提一下：我醒来第一件事就是说，我需要一个指挥中心，看清问题的范围，然后直接在 Claudeforce 里搭起来，让团队开始处理。所以我们在真实地使用它。
解读Claudeforce 的产品形态：一个装在 Claude 里的插件 = 一组 MCP 服务器 + 技能 (skills)，目前以销售场景为主 (Sales Cloud)，服务、营销等其他云还没覆盖。这说明 Claudeforce 还很早期，短期收入贡献有限。

解读这是客户亲口说的“座位风险”信号。Siemens CIO 说 Claudeforce 让销售、服务、营销人员“不再需要通过既有界面操作”，下一步写回系统后要“把人干的活拿掉”。他追求的是生产力，也就是同样的业务用更少的人或更少的 Salesforce 界面时间。如果按人头收费，生产力提升最终可能变成人头减少。目前只做“读”，Salesforce 的写入和流程自动化还没被替代。这和后面 Miguel 说“销售和服务席位还没下降”要对照着看。
Patrick Stokes、Frederik (Rik) Janssen 应用与营销总裁、Siemens (SIE) CIO (客户；对话中两人交替发言，按上下文推断) 有用
Absolutely. And there's maybe even cool things we can do together. We should have started this one before, just like I did the other prompt. But it's rocking and rolling here. This stuff is going to get... Still going. It's still going. This stuff is going to get way faster over time. Really, what's happening in the background, it's not latency within the platforms. What it is, is overthinking is what it is. It is over – right now the AI is over-reasoning over the tools that it has available to it. And so, all of that can be tuned as we move further in. And this is exactly why we want to get this out to as many customers as possible so that we can identify where you have, what should be a simple question... Yes. ...being over-reasoned on as you come back in. Another way to solve that also would be to change the model and go to something like Sonnet, which will do much less reasoning and thinking and it will just – kind of immediately the bigger the model you use, the more thinking it's going to do, which by the way is also more tokens. So, that's relevant as well.
Patrick：当然，也许我们还能一起做一些很酷的事。我们应该像刚才那个提示词一样提前开始跑这个的。它还在跑。这东西以后会快很多。后台其实不是平台的延迟，而是“想太多”：AI 现在对可用的工具推理过度。这些都可以随着推进来调优，这正是我们要尽快推给更多客户的原因，这样才能找出哪些本该简单的问题被过度推理了。另一个办法是换模型，比如换成 Sonnet，推理和思考会少得多；模型越大，思考越多，顺便说一下，token 也越多，这一点也相关。
解读两个信息：(1) Claudeforce 目前慢，原因是模型过度推理，产品还很早期；(2) Patrick 主动提到“模型越大 token 越多”，即成本问题。如果 Salesforce 将来要为客户承担模型推理成本 (比如打包进 Max 版)，选用什么模型会直接影响毛利率；这和后面 Rohan 讲自研 Koa 模型、按性价比选模型是连在一起的。

Rohan Kumar 数据与平台业务负责人 (推断：自称三个月前加入，负责数据/Informatica/Data 360/安全/Agent Fabric) 有用
And it's interesting, you look it like a lot of the value that AI is accruing, there's two very important things for these agents to be very successful. That's the model itself, which has the intelligence. It knows a lot about the world, the challenges, the model doesn't know anything about the business, right? When you look at customers, products, employees and how those interactions happen, the history and memory of how your business functions. A lot of that the model doesn't understand. So, by itself, no matter how intelligent the model is, it can't reliably reason and act on behalf of your company. And that's really where the data and the enterprise context, stuff like that, understanding of your business becomes very important. The challenge is building the enterprise context is very hard. You'd look it like the data is in silos. A lot of times the decisions that get made, they get made in meetings, there's no structured way of capturing all of that. And without having that comprehensive understanding, this is where agents hallucinate, right? When you look at how do you build trust inside agents to do complex stuff, getting that enterprise context right becomes very critical. And what I'll walk you through is, how do you go create that? And creation is one thing, but this is the most important IP of a company. And with these agents, you've got to make sure that you secure it and then you have to manage it at scale when you have like a huge workforce that's running in your enterprise.
有意思的是，AI 创造的价值里，agent 要成功有两样东西非常重要。一是模型本身，它有智能，懂很多关于世界的事。问题是模型对你的业务一无所知：客户、产品、员工以及它们之间如何互动，你的业务如何运转的历史和记忆，模型大多不理解。所以无论模型多聪明，单靠它自己无法可靠地代表你的公司推理和行动。这就是数据和企业上下文 (enterprise context)、对你业务的理解变得非常重要的地方。难点在于构建企业上下文非常难：数据分散在孤岛里，很多决策是在会议里做的，没有结构化的方式记录下来。没有这种全面理解，agent 就会产生幻觉。要让 agent 可靠地做复杂的事，把企业上下文做对非常关键。我要讲的是怎么创建它。创建是一回事，但这是公司最重要的知识产权，有了 agent 之后你必须保护它，还要在有大量数字员工运行时规模化地管理它。
解读Rohan 的核心论点：模型会商品化，“懂你业务的数据”才稀缺，而 Salesforce 掌握这些数据。这是 Salesforce 在“AI 绕过界面”之后的防守位置：入口可以在 Claude，但上下文在 Salesforce。这个论点是否成立，取决于客户的关键数据是否真的在 Salesforce 里 (而不是在 Snowflake、Databricks 等数据平台里)。
Rohan Kumar 数据与平台业务负责人 (推断：自称三个月前加入，负责数据/Informatica/Data 360/安全/Agent Fabric) 有用
So, the first step in terms of creating the enterprise context is you have to get your data ready for AI. And what I mean by that is today, if you look at any reasonably complex enterprise, they have many SaaS applications which have very high value data. They essentially have data lakes, data warehouses, storage systems, where a lot of high value data is getting stored. And it's actually really hard to even discover this, right? And so, we have this product suite called Informatica that essentially helps data leaders in an enterprise discover all their data assets, clean them, connect them, figure out the semantic meanings and make that take that first step of having the data ready for AI. So, before the agents really understand your business, it's important that our customers understand their data assets in the first place. So, that's step number one.
创建企业上下文的第一步，是让数据为 AI 做好准备。意思是，今天任何稍微复杂的企业都有很多 SaaS 应用，里面有高价值数据；还有数据湖、数据仓库、存储系统，存了大量高价值数据。光是发现这些数据就很难。所以我们有一个产品套件叫 Informatica，帮企业的数据负责人发现所有数据资产、清洗、连接、理清语义，迈出“数据为 AI 就绪”的第一步。在 agent 真正理解你的业务之前，客户首先要了解自己的数据资产。这是第一步。
解读Informatica 是 Salesforce 收购的数据管理公司 (我们 CRM - Deep 里约 4 个点的报表增速来自并购，主要是 Informatica)。这里把它放在“AI 数据准备”的第一步，是在给这笔并购找战略位置。没有给 Informatica 的收入或增速。
Rohan Kumar 数据与平台业务负责人 (推断：自称三个月前加入，负责数据/Informatica/Data 360/安全/Agent Fabric) 有用
Now, once your data is ready, it's ready. The next step essentially is to create what's called the enterprise context. And there's a difference. Data being ready means you can have like a bunch of structured data in your SQL tables that needs to be synthesized into figuring out what does revenue mean for the company, right? So, that's a higher-level concept as an example. Or what customer segmentation means for you. And to sort of create that context, we have this product called Data 360, which essentially not just relies on Salesforce data because there's obviously a lot of your enterprise data that's outside of Salesforce. And we've built this school technology called Zero Copy. And what that does is, it's able to reach into all the data systems that have been discovered by Informatica, remember the first step. And then without physically copying the data, it looks at the metadata of each of these systems and it's able to synthesize the right context in a central location. So, that's the second step. So, you've made the data ready for AI with Informatica and then Data 360 using the Zero Copy is able to get your Salesforce data and everything else into your context layer.
数据准备好之后，下一步是创建所谓的企业上下文。这两者有区别：数据就绪意味着你在 SQL 表里有一堆结构化数据，需要把它们综合成更高层的概念，比如“收入”对这家公司意味着什么，或者“客户分层”对你意味着什么。为了创建这种上下文，我们有 Data 360 这个产品，它不只依赖 Salesforce 的数据，因为企业很多数据在 Salesforce 之外。我们做了一项很酷的技术叫 Zero Copy (零拷贝)，它能接入 Informatica 发现的所有数据系统，不物理复制数据，而是查看每个系统的元数据，在一个中心位置综合出正确的上下文。这是第二步：用 Informatica 让数据为 AI 就绪，再用 Data 360 的 Zero Copy 把 Salesforce 数据和其他所有数据放进上下文层。
解读Data 360 (原 Data Cloud) 的定位：不搬数据，只读元数据，在 Salesforce 里形成“企业上下文”。Zero Copy 的好处是客户不用重复存储；对 Salesforce 收入的含义是，Data 360 按用量 (数据处理量) 收费的部分可能不会因为数据量大而大涨，因为数据还是存在 Snowflake、Databricks 等地方。这是我们的理解，原文没讲收费。
Rohan Kumar 数据与平台业务负责人 (推断：自称三个月前加入，负责数据/Informatica/Data 360/安全/Agent Fabric) 有用
Now, once you have built out the enterprise context, the third step is essentially creating your semantic model. And semantic model, essentially, is your business glossary. When you think about what does customer churn mean, what does ARR mean, what does customer help mean? These are not just some static fields that you see inside a database. These are like business metrics, business relationships that have been created over decades, which are very important, like this sort of information for an agent lands are (00:42:00) becoming very important. So, Tableau is the tool that we have, the product suite that we have that helps you create these semantic models. And it's interesting, these manifest as dashboards today, right? If you look at – Tableau's known as a BI tool, it creates dashboards. And, of course, dashboards have been commoditized right now, like every AI tool helps you create dashboards, but the value of Tableau was never the dashboard itself. It is the semantic model layer that gets manifested as a dashboard. So, in fact, if you think about, if creations of the dashboards become very common, you can do that in every AI tool. The value of your semantic models essentially has really gone up, because if your semantic model is not right, then all those dashboards are actually giving you false information. So, that's really where Tableau comes in.
构建好企业上下文之后，第三步是创建语义模型 (semantic model)。语义模型本质上就是你的业务词汇表：客户流失是什么意思，ARR 是什么意思，客户健康度是什么意思？这些不只是数据库里的静态字段，而是几十年来形成的业务指标和业务关系，对 agent 来说这类信息越来越重要。Tableau 就是帮你创建语义模型的产品套件。有意思的是，这些语义模型今天体现为仪表盘。Tableau 被认为是一个 BI 工具，用来做仪表盘。当然仪表盘现在已经商品化了，每个 AI 工具都能帮你做仪表盘，但 Tableau 的价值从来不在仪表盘本身，而在以仪表盘形式体现的语义模型层。事实上，如果做仪表盘变得很普通、每个 AI 工具都能做，语义模型的价值反而上升了，因为语义模型不对，那些仪表盘给你的就是错误信息。这就是 Tableau 的作用。
解读罕见的坦白：高管公开承认“仪表盘已经商品化”，前面 Patrick 的演示也正是 Claude 几分钟生成仪表盘。这对 Tableau (按创作者/查看者人头收费的 BI 工具) 是直接威胁。公司的应对是把 Tableau 重新定位为“语义层”。语义层有价值，但怎么收费 (是否还能按查看者人头收) 没说。看 Tableau 时要警惕查看者席位的下滑。
Rohan Kumar 数据与平台业务负责人 (推断：自称三个月前加入，负责数据/Informatica/Data 360/安全/Agent Fabric) 有用
And now you have all this trusted context, plus semantic models that you've created. It's available to all your agents. I mean, this is great because it makes the agents a lot more accurate because they're working on a deep understanding of the enterprise. And then it also makes them more efficient. So, they're not burning a lot of tokens. I think the point that Patrick was making, where if you don't have the right data organized, then the agent is spending a lot of time trying to get to the right information, which is where they're spending a lot of cycles and a lot of tokens. So, it becomes very expensive. So, creating this enterprise context early on lands are (00:43:21) becoming very important.
现在你有了可信上下文和语义模型，所有 agent 都能用。这很好，因为 agent 基于对企业的深度理解工作，会准确得多；也更高效，不会烧很多 token。正如 Patrick 说的，如果数据没组织好，agent 会花很多时间去找正确的信息，消耗大量计算周期和 token，所以会变得很贵。因此尽早建立企业上下文变得非常重要。
解读把“省 token”当卖点：好的数据层让 agent 少烧 token。对客户是省钱；对 Salesforce，如果它自己承担 Agentforce 的模型成本，也是提高毛利的手段。
Rohan Kumar 数据与平台业务负责人 (推断：自称三个月前加入，负责数据/Informatica/Data 360/安全/Agent Fabric) 有用
The challenge, of course, is once you have all this information, now if you put yourselves in the shoes as a security leader, it's extremely risky because these agents that are using this context start leaking at the leading edge, that can become a huge problem. So, this is where we introduced this product called Salesforce Guardian. It's the evolution of what we have at Trusted Services in Shield. In Salesforce, we basically had capabilities like encryption of your data, data masking, et cetera, to protect your assets. And that's evolving into two additional capabilities. One is called agent identity, which is the embodiment of how the agent runs. For very complex tasks, you don't want the agents to run on behalf of the user because that gives them a lot of privilege. You want them to have identities which can be controlled. So, that's one. And the second thing is actually protecting this enterprise context itself, like capabilities that can actually classify your data, mark things which are highly confidential and have specific data policies that actually protect those. So, that's how you secure AI.
当然挑战在于，一旦有了这些信息，站在安全负责人的角度会极其危险，因为使用这些上下文的 agent 一旦泄露数据，会是大问题。所以我们推出了 Salesforce Guardian (守护者)，它是我们原来 Shield 等可信服务的演进。在 Salesforce 里，我们原本有数据加密、数据脱敏等能力来保护资产，现在演进出两项新能力。一是 agent 身份 (agent identity)，也就是 agent 以什么身份运行。对于很复杂的任务，你不希望 agent 以用户身份运行，因为那给了它很大权限，你希望它有可控的独立身份。二是保护企业上下文本身，比如能对数据分类、标记高度机密内容、设定专门的数据策略来保护它们。这就是保护 AI 的方式。
解读Guardian = 原 Shield (安全加购产品) 的升级版，加了 agent 身份和数据分类。这是又一个可以加价的附加产品，但没给价格或是否包含在 Max 版里。agent 有独立身份这一点值得记住：如果每个 agent 都有身份，将来“按 agent 身份收费”就是一种可能的计费单位，替代按人头。这是我们的推测。
Rohan Kumar 数据与平台业务负责人 (推断：自称三个月前加入，负责数据/Informatica/Data 360/安全/Agent Fabric) 有用
And finally, again, as this digital workforce starts getting built out, you can imagine you can start with like tens of agents, hundreds, and then get into thousands of agents across your teams, platforms, applications, et cetera. And that becomes a nightmare for the CIO, for the IT leader to go manage. And this is really where for several years, IT leaders have relied on the protocol MuleSoft to manage their APIs which connect their business. And that's what we are evolving into this product called Agent Fabric. Think of it as a single registry that helps you manage all your agents, not the ones that have been built on Agentforce, but they're built on any provider, we basically are able to scan, monitor them and do things like FinOps to really look at what value you're getting out of the agents versus what you're spending on them.
最后，随着数字员工队伍的建立，可以想象从几十个 agent 开始，到几百个，再到团队、平台、应用之间成千上万个 agent，这对 CIO 和 IT 负责人来说管理起来是噩梦。多年来 IT 负责人一直依靠 MuleSoft 来管理连接业务的 API，我们正把它演进为一个叫 Agent Fabric 的产品。可以把它理解为一个统一注册中心，帮你管理所有 agent，不只是在 Agentforce 上建的，任何供应商建的都可以：我们能扫描、监控它们，还能做 FinOps，看你从 agent 得到的价值和你在它们身上的花费。
解读Agent Fabric (由 MuleSoft 演进而来) 想成为管理所有厂商 agent 的“控制台”，包括 MSFT、AMZN、GOOGL 平台上的 agent。这和 NOW 的 AI Control Tower 定位直接竞争。若成功，Salesforce 能在“别人家的 agent”上也收管理费。没有收费或客户数字。

Now, in Salesforce, like this is the stack that we showed, the bottom layer Data 360 essentially is going to evolve into this Salesforce's version of the Enterprise AI Harness, and it has six capabilities. Rohan Kumar 数据与平台业务负责人 (推断：自称三个月前加入，负责数据/Informatica/Data 360/安全/Agent Fabric) 可跳过 可信上下文 (Trusted Context) 就是我刚才讲的全部内容：综合业务理解、语义模型等。Agency and
Trusted Context is everything that I spoke about, which is synthesizing business understanding, your semantic models, all of that sort of stuff. Agency and Actions is how – and when a customer describes the outcome that they're seeking, the agent and the model, they create a plan, a sequence of steps that need to be taken to execute. That decision making comes in the agency framework. And then the Actions, the Trusted Actions is how you execute each of those steps. And every step that gets executed has to be governed, just to make sure that the agent is not doing anything that it's not supposed to do. And then Trusted Governance is essentially getting all your data policies right because that's going to influence how your Trusted Context gets created. And Security is about agent identity and data protection, right? It's a very comprehensive view that at Salesforce we're taking in terms of harness definition, not just protecting the agent, but if you can wrap your arms around the harness, you should be able to bring that with the Headless APIs to the AI of your choice. This is really where we're sort of going towards the future.
可信上下文 (Trusted Context) 就是我刚才讲的全部内容：综合业务理解、语义模型等。Agency and Actions (自主决策与执行)：当客户描述想要的结果时，agent 和模型会制定一个计划，也就是一系列要执行的步骤，这个决策发生在 agency 框架里；Trusted Actions 是怎么执行每一步，而每一步执行都必须受治理，确保 agent 不做不该做的事。Trusted Governance 是把数据策略做对，因为这会影响可信上下文如何创建。Security 是 agent 身份和数据保护。这是 Salesforce 对 harness 定义的非常全面的看法，不只是保护 agent。如果你能把 harness 整个掌握住，就应该能通过 Headless API 把它带到你选择的任何 AI 上。这是我们面向未来的方向。
Rohan Kumar 数据与平台业务负责人 (推断：自称三个月前加入，负责数据/Informatica/Data 360/安全/Agent Fabric) 有用
And an example of the model choices, what we announced in the keynote yesterday, Koa, it's actually very exciting. It's the very first CRM reasoning model from Salesforce. We've basically post-trained the open-weight Nemotron from NVIDIA, and like 27 years of deep product understanding and appreciation of the CRM workflow, more than 10 years of data and AI research have sort of been baked in. So, there's a lot of value, very excited, and this model has been designed for long-running agents. And what I mean by that is agents that can actually create a chain of thought, which has multiple steps and make decisions at each step. So, you can imagine a complex sales opportunity where what's the next best step to take to go make that happen or a very complicated service case that an agent is dealing with to get the customer to a better state. For complex situations like that, Koa can greatly help. Very, very excited about it. And then finally, evolving Agent Fabric into this AI Control Plane, which essentially helps you discover all your agents, govern them, connect multiple agents through standard protocols and eventually do your FinOps, the cost control, all sort of coming together. That's a big part.
模型选择的一个例子是我们昨天在主题演讲里发布的 Koa，非常令人兴奋。这是 Salesforce 第一个 CRM 推理模型。我们基于 NVIDIA 的开放权重模型 Nemotron 做了后训练，融入了 27 年的深度产品理解和对 CRM 工作流的认识，以及 10 多年的数据和 AI 研究，价值很大。这个模型是为长时间运行的 agent 设计的，即能形成多步思维链、在每一步做决策的 agent。比如一个复杂的销售机会，下一步最好怎么推进；或者 agent 处理的一个很复杂的服务工单，怎样让客户达到更好的状态。在这类复杂场景中，Koa 能帮很大忙。最后，Agent Fabric 会演进成这个 AI 控制平面，帮你发现所有 agent、治理它们、通过标准协议连接多个 agent，最终做 FinOps 成本控制，全部整合在一起。这是很大的一部分。
解读Koa = 基于 NVDA 开放权重 Nemotron 后训练的自有 CRM 推理模型。投资含义：(1) 降低对 Anthropic/OpenAI 等前沿模型的依赖和推理成本，有利于 Agentforce 毛利；(2) 也说明 Salesforce 在“合作 Anthropic”的同时在做替代路线。没给 Koa 的性能、成本或上线时间数字，是产品宣传。

Now, one important thing is, it's a very open and composable system. It's pretty exciting. I think this is something which is going to be very important for our customers to do their agentic transformation right. And I would love to hear your thoughts on this after the sessions. So, thank you.
重要的一点是，这是一个非常开放、可组合的系统。Salesforce 对每个挑战都会有非常全面的答案，因为我们认为没有这样完整的方案，agent 的生产部署就不会成功，所以我们采取整体的做法。但它是开放、可组合的：客户可能用别的安全厂商、别的数据厂商、别的 AI 控制平面厂商，都没关系，因为通过我们的 Headless API 可以和这些伙伴整合。这是从一开始就定下的设计原则：我们有完整方案，但你不必全用 Salesforce 的。最后我想说，虽然我们在把它演进成 harness，但构建这个产品的方式是非常渐进的。如果你是 Tableau 客户、Data 360 客户，或者是我们任何云 (Sales、Service 等) 的核心平台客户、MuleSoft 客户，你已经朝这个 harness 迈出了步子，因为我们在把这些产品的能力拆开，以非常可组合、统一的方式整合起来。很令人兴奋，我认为这对客户正确完成 agentic 转型非常重要。会后很想听听大家的想法，谢谢。
解读两点含义：(1) “不必全用 Salesforce 的”是在承认客户会混用 Snowflake、Databricks、CRWD、NOW 等其他厂商，Salesforce 不追求全栈锁定；(2) “渐进式、现有客户已经在路上”意味着 Enterprise AI Harness 主要是现有产品的重新包装和整合，不是一个可以单独定价的新产品。Rohan 整段没有任何收入、客户数或定价数字。

有意思的是，几个月后我们开始听到市场上的杂音，叫 SaaSpocalypse (SaaS 末日)，也就是 AI 在杀死 SaaS 的错误叙事。我们很好胜，心想这到底怎么回事？实际情况不是这样。幸好市场开始恢复理性。我想现在我们都相信是时候让这个错误叙事收场了，因为我们已经运营了四个多季度、一年多的业务现实是：两三年前 AI 的试点阶段结束了，这是我们告诉过大家的；企业在规模化投入生产，押注像我们这样的大软件平台，一直如此。我们把这个新阶段叫 SaaSceleration (SaaS 加速)，也有人叫复兴。核心信息是：AI 没有杀死 SaaS，事实上 AI 让 Salesforce 这样的软件厂商比以往任何时候都更重要，因为 AI 需要可信的上下文，以及安全、受治理、确定性的执行，才能在企业里创造价值。这是根本，而我们拥有你们刚才看到的全部基础设施。

Now, on the first point, I'll cover the first point. I know that you like this slide on the left side from last year. So, Robin and I, I mean, we were very confident about the business. Because of what we told you last year is the chart on the left side, the magic word, net new AOV growth. Net new AOV growth, at that point when we were here, the lines were crossing. We knew they were crossing, okay? They did cross in Q3. So, the net new AOV growth outpaced the AOV growth. Mathematically, when that happens, the AOV accelerates. AOV always grows obviously, but it was decelerating for years. Why? Because there was a lot of – because of this, because we have some years after COVID where the net new AOV was actually even negative growth and it was pulling down the AOV growth. Now, since Q3, the last 12 months, the last year, net new AOV growth has outpaced the AOV growth, okay? And we feel very confident in this net new AOV momentum going forward.
先讲第一点。我知道你们喜欢左边这张去年的图。我和 Robin 当时对业务很有信心，我们知道情况很好，连续多个季度都很强劲健康。去年我们告诉大家，会在 12 到 18 个月内看到有机订阅和支持收入再加速。猜猜谁说 12 个月、谁说 18 个月？这是合作。现在已经过去 12、13 个月了。我们给了 Q3 订阅和支持收入的指引，如果你们的模型算得对，会看到再加速的开端。为什么？因为去年我们讲的就是左边这张图的关键词：净新增 AOV (net new AOV) 增速。去年我们在这里时，两条线正在交叉，我们知道它们会交叉。它们在 Q3 交叉了，也就是净新增 AOV 增速超过了 AOV 增速。数学上，一旦如此，AOV 增速就会加快。AOV 当然一直在增长，但多年来一直在放缓，原因是疫情后有几年净新增 AOV 甚至是负增长，拖累了 AOV 增速。而自 Q3 以来，过去 12 个月、过去一年，净新增 AOV 增速一直超过 AOV 增速。我们对今后净新增 AOV 的势头很有信心。
解读最关键的“再加速”表态，但要拆开看。(1) 他说的是“有机”订阅收入再加速，却让大家看 Q3 指引“自己算”，没有直接给有机增速数字。Q3 报表增速里含 Informatica 并购贡献 (我们估计约 4 个点)，有机是否真的加速要扣掉并购后再看；我们的 CRM - Deep 测算 Q3 有机约 7%，Q2 约 6.4%。(2) 逻辑链：AOV (年度合同价值) 存量 = 去年存量 + 净新增 AOV，净新增的增速高于存量增速，存量增速就会抬头，这是数学上成立的，但幅度取决于净新增 AOV 有多大，他没给净新增 AOV 的金额或增速。(3) “过去 12 个月一直超过”是唯一的定量表述，且没有数字。CRM - Deep 的加仓条件是 Q3 有机 >=7.5%，这里的说法不足以验证。

再加上席位，也就是销售席位和服务席位。记得在 SaaSpocalypse 的世界里，世界要末日了，不会再有销售人员，不会再有客服坐席，“你们是按席位收费的模式，Salesforce 要崩溃了”。我们到现在仍然没有看到销售席位或服务席位数量的任何下降。我们当然一直在密切跟踪，因为也许某个时点会出现下降，但即便那个时点到来 (到目前为止还没来，席位还在增长)，我们也不担心，因为我们有那么多新的商业框架来实现 AI 机会的变现，我们会没事的。但到目前为止席位是在增加的。当然，Slack 席位暴涨，我认为它现在是企业软件里最热门的产品，至少在 Salesforce 内部肯定是。

Miguel Milano go-to-market 总负责人 (原 CRO，现管全部销售、客户成功和合作伙伴；头衔为推断) 必读
And then the consumption. AWUs are exponentially growing. Pretty much every quarter, we do as many AWUs. AWU is agentic work unit, which is the closest thing, is not tokens, is a closest thing to productive work delivered to the enterprise. We do more in one quarter than in the history until now. I mean, obviously mathematically, that will have to stop at some point. But is a lot of consumption, a lot of customers, Agentforce customers consistently consuming.
然后是用量。AWU 在指数级增长，几乎每个季度我们做的 AWU 都和此前历史累计一样多。AWU 是 agentic work unit (agent 工作单位)，它不是 token，而是最接近“交付给企业的生产性工作”的度量。我们一个季度做的比之前全部历史加起来还多。显然从数学上讲这总有一天会停止。但这是大量的用量、大量客户，Agentforce 客户在持续消耗。
解读AWU 是 Salesforce 自定义的用量指标 (agent 完成的一个工作单位，比如处理一个工单)。“每季度等于此前历史总和”就是每季度大约翻倍，听起来很强，但没给任何绝对数，也没说 AWU 和收入怎么挂钩。用量增长不一定等于收入增长：如果客户买的是 AELA (全公司无限量许可) 或升级到 Max 版里包含的额度，用量翻倍可能不产生额外收入。本场 (第一部分) 没有给 Agentforce ARR (我们的口径约 15 亿美元)。

解读这是本段对商业模式最有冲击力的一句：公司的营收总负责人亲口说“我不再登录 Salesforce，但用得比以前多 100 倍”。这正是我们 CRM - Deep 的核心矛盾：价值 (使用量) 在上升，但传统按“登录用户”计价的基础在动摇。Salesforce 目前的答案是把 Claudeforce 打包进按人头收费的 Max 版 (550 美元/人/月)，即仍然按人收，只是每人更贵。如果客户的人数减少，或者客户认为“不登录就不该付整个席位的钱”，这个模式就要转向按用量 (MCP 调用、AWU) 收费，而那部分目前没定价。“100 倍”是口语夸张，不是数据。

我来帮大家理解这张图上的三件事，讨论会比较密集。第一，我们认为 AI 变现的机会非常大，TAM (可服务市场总量) 在爆发。那个我们不再提的词 (SaaSpocalypse) 并没有烧掉软件的机会，反而扩大了它。Robin 在她的部分会讲一些硬数字，以及我们看到它如何分布在行业和客户之间。一个有意思的思考方式是知识工作者的扩张：今天全球大约有 10 亿知识工作者，我们平台上大约有 5,000 万。还有巨大的上升空间去拿下相当大一部分市场，而这还没算把 agent 当作知识工作者，我认为那会让知识工作者的基数成几何级扩大。我们能做的事太多了。这是第一点。
解读TAM 论述：全球约 10 亿知识工作者，Salesforce 平台上约 5,000 万 (约 5% 渗透)。这是一个按“人”算的 TAM，说明公司仍然把人头作为主要的收费基础，并设想 agent 也会成为“类人头”的计费对象。“把 agent 当知识工作者”如果意味着将来按 agent 数量收费，就是对席位收缩的对冲，但没给任何定价。TAM 的具体数字留给 Robin (第二部分)。

解读“builder”是一种更偏技术的售前/交付人员，帮客户当场搭原型。这说明 AI 产品的销售需要更重的技术投入，获客成本更高。1,000 人是今年年底的规模。客户“帮我建”的需求很强，但也意味着 Claudeforce 这类产品还不能“开箱即用”。

I think though, and Replit's a great example of this, as they expanded their core sales and service seats, they started to think about agents. Alexa Vignone 总裁兼首席营收官 (President & CRO，新任) 必读
So, I think it's a really powerful example of how customers can start with their internal use cases, but they don't stop there. I think once they understand the power of an agentic capability, they start to think about, gosh, I have to get that in front of my customer. And CrowdStrike is another really good example. We implemented a whole bunch of agents, but the two biggest agents were an ask legal agent and – they follow MEDDPICC, so they do sort of like a qualification process and they wanted to help boost their sort of pipeline quality. So, we launched those two internal agents for them. Fast forward, they now have multiple agents in production, but they now run an external agent, their service agent and that is containing 83% of the inbound requests that come to their contact center. You can't actually file a support ticket without touching Agentforce when you talk to CrowdStrike.
这很好地说明了客户如何从内部场景开始，但他们不会止步于此。一旦理解了 agentic 能力的威力，他们就会想，我得把它放到客户面前。CrowdStrike 是另一个很好的例子，我和 CrowdStrike 合作了五六年。他们开始 agentic 之旅时是 Sales Cloud 和 Service Cloud 客户。我们实施了一大批 agent，其中两个最大的是“问法务”agent，以及一个资格评估 agent，因为他们遵循 MEDDPICC 销售方法论，要做商机资格评估，想提升 pipeline 质量。我们为他们上线了这两个内部 agent。快进到现在，他们有多个 agent 在生产环境，而且运行了一个对外的服务 agent，处理了进入他们呼叫中心的 83% 的请求。现在你找 CrowdStrike 提交支持工单，不可能不经过 Agentforce。
解读CRWD 案例：对外服务 agent 自主处理 (containment) 了 83% 的进线请求。这对 Agentforce 是好的成功案例，但反过来看，这正是“服务坐席会减少”的直接证据：83% 的请求不再需要人工客服，CRWD 需要的 Service Cloud 坐席理论上会下降。管理层把它讲成成功故事，却没说 CRWD 的服务席位数变化，也没说 Agentforce 的收入能否覆盖可能流失的席位收入。

So, I think that's a really powerful example of the pattern. Alexa Vignone 总裁兼首席营收官 (President & CRO，新任) 必读
And then finally and this is a little bit of a departure, it's a super new use case. I'm being sort of transparent and probably leaning in a little bit to an opportunity that we see from a monetization perspective that's really, really new. I love it for a lot of reasons. And as people launch their agents into Slack, what they need is a super high latency performant pipeline to make sure that they are fueling their agents with Slack data. I feel really comfortable about the seat expansion opportunity that we have, but this breaks that association. I monitor every digital native and how they use our Slack system today. 最后这一个有点不同，是一个非常新的场景。我坦白说，我可能有点超前地在讲一个从变现角度看非常非常新的机会，我很喜欢它，原因很多。第一，在第二季度初我们还没看到这种商机，它是在过去 90 天里出现的。我们现在看到的是 Slack 正在成为 agent 的“agentic 操作系统”。随着人们把 agent 部署进 Slack，他们需要一个超高性能 (原文为 high latency，按语境应为低延迟高性能) 的数据管道，确保用 Slack 数据为 agent 提供燃料。我对这个非常兴奋。我们大概已经成交了三笔，还有很多在 pipeline 里，目标主要是数字原生公司。我喜欢的一点是它打破了与席位的关联。Miguel 讲了席位，我对席位扩张机会很放心，但这打破了那个关联。我监控每一家数字原生公司今天怎么用 Slack，一个很令人兴奋的现象是 API 调用和使用量并不跟员工人数挂钩：一家规模很小但增长很快的公司可以疯狂地建 agent，却不需要很多员工。
解读这是第一部分里最“新”的变现信息：按 Slack 数据 API 访问 (给外部 agent 用的数据管道) 收费，和员工人数脱钩。但规模很小：过去 90 天才出现，目前只成交约 3 笔，没给金额和定价方式。意义在方向而非数字：这是 Salesforce 第一次明确讲出一种“不按人头”的收费，和 Headless/MCP 调用变现的逻辑相同。CRM - Deep 说 Headless 360/MCP 调用尚未变现，这里算是一个早期例外，值得跟踪。

最后，我们在考虑更多的购买方式。你们可能会说，你们的购买方式已经很多了。但我认为这对我们非常重要：我们要在客户所处的位置满足他们，确保他们有非常灵活、摩擦很低的方式接入平台。看 Q2，我们最大的 10 笔交易里有 7 笔是 AELA。我认为随着 Salesforce Commit 在团队中更全面地推出，它也会成为我们收入加速的主要驱动力。
解读定价/打包关键段。(1) AELA (Agentic Enterprise License Agreement，全公司 agent 使用的一揽子许可，类似“自助餐包年”) 占 Q2 前 10 大交易的 7 笔，说明大客户越来越多用“一口价包年”买 AI，而不是按用量付费。好处是合同金额大、锁定期长 (也解释了前面说的合同期限变长)；坏处是用量 (AWU) 再怎么涨，收入也被锁在一口价里，用量增长和收入增长脱钩。(2) Salesforce Commit 是新的承诺消费式购买方式 (客户先承诺一笔总额，再在各产品间灵活使用，类似云厂商的承诺消费)，被说成“收入加速的主要驱动力”，但没给任何数字和推出时间表。没提 Flex Credits。

解读我们 CRM - Deep 的“B 型增长” (新 SKU 驱动提价) 在这里得到最直接的确认。(1) “基数每升级 1% = 1 亿美元”是新的量化口径：按我们的理解，这意味着可升级的核心 CRM 基数加上升级溢价 (后面说 60% 到 80%) 大约对应 120 亿到 170 亿美元的存量合同；每年如果升级 5% 的基数就是约 5 亿美元增量，大约贡献 1 个多点的总收入增速 (我们的测算，非公司数字)。(2) 十多年没有新版本，说明 Max 版是一次真正的提价周期。(3) Headless (也就是 Claudeforce 的底层能力) 放在 Max 版里，印证了 Claudeforce 的变现路径是“升级版本”，不是按调用收费。

This is the execution point. Everyone is going to be focused on monetizing the value addition.
我认为这会非常强大，我们已经让合作伙伴和整个公司围绕推动升级周期来组织，并确保和客户一起让他们在升级后取得成功。最后再说一点：刚才我讲了八个季度，坦白说在这八个季度里我们只是部分地重视这件事，就已经做出了 10 亿美元的 AOV。看机制的话，升级时根据客户情况和原来所在的版本，大约有 60% 到 80% 的价格提升。但看单个客户层面的数据，我们看到的是 ARR 扩大到 1.4 倍。这非常有力，第一，我们在增长，这是核心信息。但客户续约时，你们可以算一算，ARR 的增幅会比升级费用略少一些：他们在重新调配产品组合，重新对齐到他们需要去的方向，为增长和转型配置所需的产品。所以这不仅带来营收增长，我认为还降低了未来流失的担忧。我们会调动公司全部力量，从市场营销和活动、到赋能计划、项目、激励、到一线的行动号召。这是执行的关键，所有人都将专注于把价值版本变现。
解读最重要的数字段之一。(1) 8 个季度升级累计 10 亿美元 AOV，约每年 5 亿美元。(2) 关键的“打折”信息：升级的标价提升是 60% 到 80%，但客户层面的 ARR 只扩大到 1.4 倍 (+40%)，她自己说续约时 ARR 增幅“比升级费用略少”，因为客户在“重新调配产品组合”，也就是升级 Max 的同时砍掉了一些别的产品或席位。生活例子：手机套餐从 100 元升到 170 元，但同时退掉了另一张副卡，总账单只从 100 元涨到 140 元。这说明升级的净增量大约只有标价提升的一半到三分之二。(3) 她把这说成“降低流失担忧”，实际含义是升级在部分程度上是用更高单价换更少的产品/席位，这和我们“单客户账单能否在席位收缩时上升”的问题直接相关：能上升，但幅度被稀释。

(Pierre) 好，我们用你们的 CRM 已经 10 到 15 年了。还不错，但没什么特别的。我们有合作关系，但不是 (希望你喜欢这个采访) 不是战略关系，也不是战略伙伴。我们有点卡住了：有 42 个 Salesforce 实例 (instance)，很难管理，维护成本高等等。我们得搭很复杂的机制才能看到全局。而且像 Amazon (AMZN)、Siemens 这样的全球客户越来越多地要求我们在全世界用同一套流程，好给他们出全球报表。后来我们来了 Dreamforce，和你们的团队一起决定上这项技术 (Data Cloud)。这对我们是转变的基础，因为不到三个月，我们就把所有数据放到了一层上，可以跑报表了。(Miguel) 那大概是三四年前？(Pierre) 两年半。两年半。
解读Adecco 的起点：42 个 Salesforce 实例，相当于一家公司开了 42 个互不相通的账本。Data Cloud (数据云) 的价值是把这些账本在上面合成一层，三个月就能出全球报表。这说明 Salesforce 的 AI 故事在大客户那里，第一步通常是先卖数据层，Data Cloud 是后面 Agentforce 的前置条件。

解读这段是少见的坦白：管理层自己承认，在 AI 之前，一个八位数 (年付 $10M 以上) 的大客户在谈缩减用量，Salesforce 面临流失。这正是我们 CRM - Deep 里说的问题：老产品在大客户里已经饱和，数量 (坐席) 增长没有了。Data Cloud 先“止血”，Agentforce 再扩张，这是他们要讲的样板故事。对投资判断的含义是：样板客户的起点是“差点缩水”，说明没有 AI 新产品时，存量客户的自然趋势是持平甚至下降。

(Pierre) 几个月前我们和 Parker (Salesforce 联合创始人 Parker Harris) 去了巴黎郊区的一个分支机构，Parker 很友好地拜访了分支，和我们的招聘顾问聊天。我们一位女招聘顾问告诉 Parker，这个 agent 改变了她的生活，因为晚上 5 点 50 分下班前，她把所有 agent 启动，让它们夜里干活。第二天早上回来，她不用再给 200 个候选人打电话，agent 已经预审好了 20 个候选人。这个 agent 的一大优势是它一周 7 天、一天 24 小时运行。我们的分支关门时，我们仍在做业务，预审候选人。(Miguel) 实际上 agent 的很大一部分工作是在下班时间完成的，对吧？(Pierre) 是的。

解读价值证明：每周 2 万场语音面试、满足率提升 10%。AWU (Agentic Work Unit，agent 完成一次工作的计量单位，类似电表读数) 只说“数百万级”，没给精确数，也没说对应多少收费。这是本场为数不多的客户侧量化数字，但它证明的是客户获得的价值，Salesforce 拿到多少要看合同 (下面会讲到是 AELA 包年，用量不额外收钱)。

解读关键信息：Adecco 把 Coworker (Salesforce 新的对话式界面，像在 Lightning 里嵌了一个 Claude Cowork) 推给 2.7 万名用户，而且是“直接打开开关”，不需要实施项目。两个含义：(1) Adecco 3.4 万员工里有 2.7 万是 Salesforce 用户，坐席渗透率很高，这块坐席本身已经没有多少增长空间；(2) 新界面不需要实施费，对 Salesforce 的专业服务收入和合作伙伴生态都不是好事，但上线快意味着升级可以更快推广。

解读定价信号，必读。(1) Coworker 只对 Max 版 ($550/用户/月) 或 A1E (我们的理解是 Agentforce 1 Edition，旧的顶配版) 开放，这是用新功能逼客户升级版本的典型做法，对应我们说的增长类型 B (新产品带价格)。(2) 但 Adecco 已经买了 A1E，所以打开 Coworker 没有带来新收入，Miguel 自己说原以为“这季度要签大单”，结果没有。(3) “三年前大数字在下降”再次确认 AI 之前这个客户在缩水；“显著扩大”没给数字。含义：升级周期的收入要看还有多少客户停在 Core/Advanced 低版本，已经在顶配的客户再推新界面不会多付钱。

解读AELA (Agentic Enterprise License Agreement，企业级 agent 包年协议) 的真实样子：两年期、所有用例无限额度，像自助餐包月。对 Salesforce 的含义是：签约时一次性抬高合同额 (后面 Miguel 说 Adecco “不太情愿”签了第一份 AELA，花费上了一个台阶)，但两年里用量涨多少都不多收钱，AWU 数百万次、Coworker 2.7 万用户都不带来增量收入，要等续约时再“分享价值”。这份合同 7 个月前签，续约大约在 2028 年初 (我们的推算)。所以 AELA 客户的用量增长对收入是滞后的，而且续约时客户会拿 AI 节省的人力反过来压价，Miguel 在台上已经被当面提醒要“照顾好”。

(Paul) 这是那种客户自发创造、主动要求、几乎从我们手里抢过去的好产品。就像你说的，当时是 Kate 在内部这么做。早在 Salesforce in Claude 成为今天这个产品之前，我们就已经在 Claude 里用 Salesforce 了。然后很多我们共同的客户也在这么做。你们在 Salesforce 内部也开始这么做了。(Miguel) 甚至在 Claudeforce 之前？(Paul) 对。但那时很笨拙，要自己去连接。因为直觉上这就很合理：如果我在 Claude 和 Cowork 这样的环境里工作，我就想访问那里的数据。怎么把数据拉进来？怎么按我想要的方式分析？怎么双向读写，并且以很强大的方式使用它？有了 Mark 说的 Claudeforce，也就是我们说的 Salesforce Claude，就是把所有连接器 (connector) 和技能 (skill) 都做好，去掉所有毛刺，让整个过程变得无缝。
解读Paul 讲清了产品本质：Claudeforce 就是一套连接器加技能，让用户在 Claude 里双向读写 Salesforce 数据。打个比方，以前要进 Salesforce 的“营业厅”办事，现在在 Claude 这个“手机 App”里就能办。连接器本身技术门槛不高 (客户自己也能凑合着连)，Salesforce 的护城河在于数据、权限和流程，而不是界面。

我深入讲讲框架的最后一个支柱：资本配置。我们有两个关键原则：一是负责任的并购和战略投资，二是稳健的资本回报，我相信作为股东你们都非常关心后者。这是我之前讲的框架，仍然有效，仍然是我们考虑投资、收购以及放弃哪些收购的方式。最近我想讲几个关键关注领域：数据、AI 加速器，以及一个新方向 AI Labs。我们评估这些机会时看的是：技术和人才、相邻领域、可扩展性。技术和人才方面，我要提一下 Doti。这笔收购是我们的并购团队和 Slack 团队共同发现的机会，是你们在 Slack 里看到的一些创新的关键组成部分。最右边，我们讲过 Slack 和 Informatica，它们显然给我们带来了很好的顺风，帮助推动 FY2030 框架里的增长。
解读CFO 明确说 Slack 和 Informatica 是 FY30 框架增长的“顺风”，也就是 $63B 里有一块明确靠并购。并购关注的方向是数据、AI 加速器和新设的 AI Labs，说明并购这台发动机还会继续开，这和“增长质量”问题直接相关：未来报表增速里并购贡献可能长期存在。

Robin Washington 总裁兼首席运营与财务官 (COO/CFO) 必读
The other component that I mentioned is capital return. And I think the biggest bet that we've made in FY 2027 is on us. We've been buying shares for long term, $60 billion cumulative. But we launched earlier this year and we'll complete in October, the largest accelerated share repurchase ever. The return over 40%, the expected average price, $182 a share. The expected share count reduction over 14%. And again, that brings value to all of you, our shareholders. That is in addition to the $3 billion in dividends that we paid out.
我提到的另一部分是资本回报。我认为我们在 FY2027 下的最大赌注就是押在我们自己身上。我们一直在长期回购股票，累计 $60B。但今年早些时候我们启动了有史以来最大的加速股票回购 (ASR)，会在 10 月完成。回报超过 40%，预期平均价格每股 $182，预期股本减少超过 14%。这为你们股东带来了价值。此外我们还支付了 $3B 股息。
解读回购段，必读。(1) 史上最大 ASR (加速回购：先向投行一次性拿回一大批股票，投行再在市场上慢慢买回来平仓)，10 月完成，预期均价 $182/股，股本减少超过 14%。按我们的粗算 (股本约 9.6 亿股)，规模在 $20B 以上。(2) “回报超过 40%”原文没讲清口径，我们的理解是按当时股价相对 $182 均价的账面收益。(3) 累计回购 $60B，另付股息 $3B。(4) 股本减少 14% 会直接把每股收益和每股 FCF 抬高约 16%，这是 FY27 到 FY28 每股指标的重要来源，与经营增长无关。后面 Benioff 透露 ASR 有借债，打算卖 Anthropic 股票还债。
Robin Washington 总裁兼首席运营与财务官 (COO/CFO) 必读

总结一下，我们去年讲的框架保持不变。我们对已经讲过的打法、创新、go-to-market 策略以及通往 $63B 的路径都非常有信心。信心在，TAM 也在。最后总结一下，好让我们进入问答：我们相信我们正在赢得 agentic enterprise 的机会。我们在定义 agentic enterprise。执行正在让我们的价值复利增长，正在释放被困住的价值。正如我所说，机会是我们的。我们相信我们带来的创新会继续推动飞轮。谢谢。
解读结论：“框架保持不变”。也就是说，FY30 $63B (含 Informatica) 不上调也不下调，没有新增有机目标，没有新增利润率或 FCF 的长期数字。在 AI 叙事这么强的一场投资者日上不上调目标，可以理解为管理层自己对有机再加速的幅度也没把握，只敢维持。

Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 可跳过 Rami 当着这家公司 CEO 的面，开始演示他自己版本的 Claudeforce，他还起了个名字。他构建了一个高度定制
And Rami was in front of the CEO of this company and started demonstrating his version of Claudeforce, he'd give it a name. He had built this highly customized application to run Salesforce, Switzerland, and I will never forget this, but the customers' jaw literally dropped open when they saw what was happening, which was that the platform was able to kind of read across our entire database, all of our applications and build this highly customized application that let Rami run his business. And this was kind of an amazing moment.
Rami 当着这家公司 CEO 的面，开始演示他自己版本的 Claudeforce，他还起了个名字。他构建了一个高度定制的应用来运营 Salesforce 瑞士，我永远不会忘记，客户当场惊得下巴都掉了。他们看到的是：平台能读遍我们整个数据库、所有应用，构建出这个高度定制的应用让 Rami 管理他的业务。这是一个很了不起的时刻。
解读故事的高潮：业务负责人不靠 IT，自己在 Claude 里拼出一个读遍全公司 Salesforce 数据的应用。注意这里的价值实现靠的是 Claude 界面加 Salesforce 数据，Salesforce 收费依据仍不清楚。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用
It was only complemented by my own personal experience was we were in the focus groups for the Dreamforce keynote that you saw yesterday and Miguel, who's right here, we were in Beverly Hills and we're going through – we have customers there. We're presenting to them. I'm sure you know, what we do. It's very exciting. And then we're trying to listen them. And I just kind of glance over and I look at Miguel, and I see that he has taken Claudeforce and he is running an application that looked unlike anything I had ever seen. He was incredible graphical, dynamic, very interactive, very intelligent. And he was kind of switching between that and Slack and so forth. And it just all of a sudden occurred to me everything is really changing because of what is happening at the interface lever (02:27:12), and at this interface layer, we're really seeing this kind of transformational moment. So this was the thought that and I don't know if I'm going to be able to express this correctly, but there are certain moments in our industry where everything is changing. And this is that moment for enterprise. We saw it when we were doing IBM mainframes and then we went into minicomputers. But then all of a sudden we started to go into client server computing. We saw it when we were moving client server computing, and then all of a sudden we were doing cloud computing, and now we're moving from cloud computing and we're moving into this new platform.
我自己的亲身经历更加印证了这一点。我们在为你们昨天看到的 Dreamforce 主题演讲做焦点小组，Miguel 就在这儿，我们在比佛利山庄，那里有客户，我们给他们演示。你们知道我们是怎么做的，非常令人兴奋。然后我们试着听他们的意见。我一转头看 Miguel，发现他用 Claudeforce 跑着一个我从没见过的应用。它图形化、动态、交互性强、非常智能。他在这个和 Slack 之间来回切换。我突然意识到，一切都在变，因为界面层正在发生的事情，在这个界面层，我们真的看到了一个转型时刻。所以我的想法是 (不知道能不能表达准确)：我们这个行业有一些时刻是一切都在变的，这就是企业软件的那个时刻。我们在做 IBM (IBM) 大型机、然后进入小型机时见过；后来突然进入客户端/服务器计算；然后从客户端/服务器走向云计算；现在我们正从云计算走向这个新平台。
解读Benioff 把现在定性为和“大型机到小型机、客户端/服务器到云”同级别的平台迁移，界面层在变。历史上每次平台迁移，老的应用厂商都有被替代的风险 (Salesforce 自己就是云迁移的赢家)。他讲这个是为了表明自己在新平台上先行一步，但也等于承认旧的“登录 Salesforce 界面、按人头收费”的模式正在被改写。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用
And that's why yesterday I kind of went through these four strategic layers of how our software is architected. And for those of you who've been coming into these Dreamforce's and going through these things, you'll see we really haven't had to kind of spell it out quite like that, the data layer where the data is integrated and federated and harmonized, the application and semantic layer, which before we were just talking about the applications and the power of the applications, but now we're talking about how the applications and the analytics provide the intelligence for the AI, the agentic layer which we have been talking about, Agentforce, but not as part of the comprehensive platform and now a transformational interface. And that transformational interface I think is why I think for a lot of customers, as you kind of travel around, they'll say, they really haven't had their enterprise AI transformation. I don't think most companies feel and probably, a lot of companies are represented in the room that when you go to work, you feel like, oh yeah, AI has really changed everything in our company. Maybe people are still having their ChatGPT moment at home or on their phones, but when they get to their office, are they feeling like, yeah, wow, everything is different in our company, but it's extremely clear to me and I'm very confident that we are now at that moment where all of a sudden we are going to see a rapid transformation of the enterprise. And what we're doing with all of these customers today is showing them how to kind of guide that transformation.
这就是为什么我昨天讲了我们软件架构的四个战略层。常来 Dreamforce 的人会发现，我们以前从没这样把它讲清楚过：数据层，数据在这里被集成、联邦化、统一；应用和语义层，以前我们只讲应用和应用的力量，现在我们讲应用和分析如何为 AI 提供智能；agent 层，我们一直在讲 Agentforce，但以前没有作为完整平台的一部分来讲；现在还有一个变革性的界面层。我认为这个变革性界面就是为什么你走访各地时，很多客户会说他们还没有经历企业 AI 转型。我不认为大多数公司 (在座很多公司也是) 去上班时会觉得 AI 真的改变了公司的一切。也许大家还在家里或手机上经历他们的 ChatGPT 时刻，但到了办公室，他们会觉得公司里一切都不一样了吗？但我非常清楚、也非常有信心，我们现在正处在这样一个时刻：突然之间，我们会看到企业的快速转型。我们今天和所有这些客户做的，就是告诉他们如何引导这场转型。
解读四层架构 (数据层、应用与语义层、agent 层、界面层) 是理解 Salesforce 新叙事的骨架。Benioff 承认大多数企业“还没有经历企业 AI 转型”，这也解释了为什么 Agentforce ARR 只有约 $1.5B (相对 $40B 以上的收入盘子还很小)。“快速转型”没有给时间或数字。

Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用
One of the reasons why it's going to go so fast is because those customers as they start to deploy this interface, the interface is so intelligent, it's helping those customers implement it with them. 进展会这么快的原因之一是，客户开始部署这个界面时，界面本身非常智能，它在帮客户一起实施。所以 Miguel 或 Rami 身边都没有庞大的技术团队来实施这项技术。这不是那种庞大的企业转型项目，但你最终会得到一场企业转型。界面本身足够聪明，能做三件关键的事。第一，它能帮助管理 Salesforce。这非常重要，因为 Salesforce 有成千上万种不同的配置项，所以管理员得进去在不同的管理中心里操作。第二，它让他们能构建这些应用。第三，它让他们能运营这些应用。所以业务单元负责人或业务负责人，比如 Miguel 或 Rami，突然就有了这种不可思议的能力。
解读“界面能帮你管理、构建、运营 Salesforce”意味着实施难度和实施费用下降。好处是新功能推广更快、升级更容易；坏处是 Salesforce 管理员 (也是付费坐席) 和合作伙伴实施生态的需求下降。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用
And I think we are really uniquely positioned to offer this to customers for like one really important reason. And I think that you can kind of see how it kind of played out in that. I think I did a tweet earlier this year about Salesforce Headless and all of a sudden, this Headless idea went super viral. But while that happened, I was actually kind of shocked because it seemed to me Salesforce has always been Headless. People were excited that we had an MCP API. Well, we had to had an XML API, a SOAP API, a REST API. It's just a maturation of our API platform. And the other key characteristic beside that we have a – were built on a robust API strategy and we always have is that our applications, especially the ones that are built in lightning, lightning is not just a runtime environment, it's a design environment. So those customers have designed those applications and have decomposed them into the metadata. The metadata is stored for all those customers in the database.
我认为我们处在非常独特的位置向客户提供这些，原因很重要，你们大概能看出来是怎么发生的。今年早些时候我发了一条关于 Salesforce Headless (无头) 的推文，突然这个 Headless 的概念就疯传了。但我其实有点震惊，因为在我看来 Salesforce 一直都是 Headless 的。大家兴奋于我们有 MCP API。可我们以前就有 XML API、SOAP API、REST API，这只是我们 API 平台的成熟。除了建立在稳健的 API 战略上之外，另一个关键特点是：我们的应用，尤其是用 Lightning 构建的应用，Lightning 不只是运行环境，还是设计环境。所以客户设计了这些应用，并把它们分解成元数据 (metadata)。所有客户的元数据都存储在数据库里。
解读Headless (无头) 的意思是不需要 Salesforce 自己的界面，通过 API 或 MCP (让 AI 调用外部系统的标准协议) 直接调用数据和功能。Benioff 说“我们一直都是 Headless”，强调技术优势：应用以元数据 (描述应用长什么样、有哪些字段和规则的数据) 存在数据库里，AI 可以读懂并重组。我们的看法：技术上是优势，但 Headless 也意味着用户不登录 Salesforce 界面，按登录坐席收费的逻辑会被削弱，而 MCP 调用目前没有收费。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用
So to your point before that metadata then is recomposed into browsers today, into HTML browsers, Safari, Chrome, Mozilla, whatever, we don't have a religious choice up there either. Now the browser level is transformed and instead of having that level you have Cowork or you're all going to have other opportunities up here. Quite a few I'm sure that let you build and chat and create and so forth. And now that kind of very call it an intelligent browser, it can read through that same metadata and then recompose those applications. And because our platform knows what the applications look like, what the data is, the user models, the sharing models, it is informed by the semantic layer as well. Instantly you get that application. That is something I've never seen before in my career. I don't think anybody's ever seen this. I think it's by far the most exciting thing I've ever seen. 说到你之前的问题，这些元数据今天会被重新组合到浏览器里，HTML 浏览器，Safari、Chrome、Mozilla，不管哪个，我们在那一层也没有“宗教偏好”。现在浏览器这一层变了，取而代之的是 Cowork，或者你们还会有很多其他的选择，我相信会有不少，让你构建、聊天、创作等等。现在这种可以叫做“智能浏览器”的东西，能读取同样的元数据，然后重新组合出这些应用。因为我们的平台知道应用长什么样、数据是什么、用户模型、共享模型，还有语义层的信息，你瞬间就能得到那个应用。这是我职业生涯里从没见过的。我想没人见过。这绝对是我见过最激动人心的事。完全出乎意料，客户能得到的价值会非常惊人。所以我昨天做演讲时无比兴奋。我觉得，哇，这真是一个戏剧性的时刻，而我们在这个时刻处于独特的位置。我有信心，不知道你们在座有多少公司用 Salesforce 运营业务，有一些吧，这对你们是立刻就可以打开的机会，不用等。你们会像 Miguel 或 Rami 今天这样生活在那个环境里，很快会有数千个客户这样。
解读答案收尾。Benioff 把 Claude/Cowork 比作新一代“智能浏览器”，Salesforce 是它后面的元数据和数据平台。这个比喻本身就说明了风险：浏览器时代，网站不向浏览器收钱，也不靠浏览器收钱，而是靠自己的业务模式。整段回答 (约 1,900 词) 没有回应 Brad 的核心问题：从别人前门进来时如何变现、黏性如何量化。“立刻就可以打开”对应 Coworker 需要 Max 版或 A1E，实质是升级推销。

解读Benioff 的核心论点：绝大多数企业会通过打包软件 (packaged software，比如 Salesforce、SAP (SAP)、Microsoft (MSFT)) 购买 AI，而不是自己挑模型、自己搭。这是应用软件公司“不会被模型公司吃掉”的关键假设。反方证据就在今天的台上：Anthropic 自己的 15 亿次操作是在 Claude 界面里完成的。这个判断是否成立，决定了 AI 支出有多少会流到 Salesforce。

所以我看到的是，客户真正想要的是一场企业 AI 转型。他们能看到有技术可以让他们的生产力和成功程度大幅提升。就像他们在个人生活里体验到的那样，可以把一些个人数据放进这些模型，得到某种洞察。他们希望用这个来运营业务。我们知道这是可能的，因为我们自己现在就是这么做的。如果你去和 Miguel 待一会儿，你应该把他拉到一边，或者任何一位高管，你会看到那种不可思议的体验。我有信心，我们现在拥有基于 AI 的、高度差异化的客户体验，处于可能性的绝对顶峰。我想我们昨天展示了这一点，这些客户现在有了一个绝对真实的北极星，知道如何带领公司前进。这些公司在 Salesforce 上投了数十亿美元，比如我反复提的瑞士那个例子，那家公司在部署 Salesforce 上花了数十亿美元，它会因此上到一个全新的水平。我们知道这一点的唯一原因是我们自己已经做到了。我认为因为我们在平台、界面、Slack 以及作为“零号客户”所做的其他事情，我们有了更高的生产力和更强的能力。我们会尝试为每一个客户做到这一点。这说得通。我真的很想直接回应零数据保留的问题，所以很高兴你给了我这个机会。谢谢。
解读收尾是宣传话术：客户想要企业 AI 转型，Salesforce 自己是“零号客户”。“生产力更高”这点要和 Salesforce 自身减少客服人员的做法一起看：如果客户也像 Salesforce 自己这样用 AI 提效，买的坐席会变少。

问题 3：Alex Zukin 分析师 (Wolfe Research)

Alex Zukin 分析师 (Wolfe Research) 必读
Thank you, Kirk. Let's go to Alex in the front. Hey, Marc. Alex Zukin with Wolfe Research. Truly inspiring presentation on all fronts. When we talk to customers and we do a lot of survey work, we do a lot of direct questioning. I think the main question is how much does it cost? And from the vantage point, you're putting out a truly differentiated amount of innovation and functionality, you're also being very flexible on how you price it SKUs (02:43:42), consumption, value-based outcomes. When you think about the distribution of how much value goes to the model versus goes to the harness or the app. In the future or over the course of the next few years, how much of that can you capture and how do you explain that to customers of what they should be willing to pay for Salesforce?
(Benioff) 谢谢 Kirk。请前排的 Alex。(Alex) 嗨 Marc，我是 Wolfe Research 的 Alex Zukin。各方面的演讲都非常鼓舞人心。我们和客户交流、做大量调研、直接提问时，我认为最主要的问题是：要花多少钱？从你们的角度，你们推出了真正差异化的创新和功能，在定价上也非常灵活：SKU、按用量、基于价值的结果计费。你怎么看价值在模型和 harness (把模型接到业务里的那层“缰绳”)/应用之间的分配？未来几年你们能拿走多少？你们怎么向客户解释他们应该愿意为 Salesforce 付多少钱？

Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 必读
Yeah, I think that one of the most interesting things that's happening and this is a challenge from Miguel, so he should probably weigh in here and the word (02:44:12), how I've tried to influence him. I'll tell you my own personal narrative, which is that every customer wants something slightly different on pricing, some customers want per user pricing. It gives them predictability. They understand the cost structure. It makes sense to them. Some customers want per agent pricing, some customers want consumption pricing, some customers want usage pricing, some customers want transaction outcome pricing that is, I've completed the transaction, therefore I'm paying for that. And some customers want business outcome pricing, which I've never seen before, but now they're like, if you save me so much money, I'll give you a percentage to that. If you make this much money, I'll give you a percentage to that. No one majority of customers can you drop in any one of these buckets. So what we've said to our entire sales organization is we are giving you ultimate flexibility to write the best deal for that customer. We want Miguel to know that he can walk into a customer and say and Miguel has a very large distribution organization, you know, the size and scale of it. And basically not be constrained in any way and say this is the right price for you. That is the way to get the most value. And over and over again, you have to remember, we have many different ways to get value. One is that agreement that we're writing, another is also fundamentally reducing attrition like we've done also increases our value dramatically to our shareholders.
是的，我认为正在发生的最有意思的事情之一，这是 Miguel 面对的挑战，所以他也许应该来讲讲，我也试图影响他。我讲讲我自己的看法：每个客户在定价上要的都略有不同。有些客户要按用户定价，这让他们有可预测性，他们理解成本结构，觉得合理。有些客户要按 agent 定价，有些要按消耗定价，有些要按用量定价，有些要按交易结果定价，也就是我完成了交易所以付钱。还有些客户要按业务结果定价，这是我以前从没见过的，现在他们会说：如果你帮我省了这么多钱，我分你一个百分比；如果你帮我赚了这么多钱，我分你一个百分比。没有哪一种能装下大多数客户。所以我们对整个销售组织说：我们给你最大的灵活性，为客户写出最好的交易。我们希望 Miguel 知道，他可以走进一个客户，Miguel 有一个非常庞大的销售组织，你知道它的规模，完全不受限制地说：这是适合你的价格。这是获取最大价值的方式。而且你一定要记住，我们有很多不同的获取价值的方式。一是我们签的协议，另一个是从根本上降低流失率，像我们已经做到的那样，这也大大提高了我们对股东的价值。
解读Benioff 没有回答“价值在模型和应用之间怎么分、Salesforce 能拿多少”，而是列举了六种定价方式 (按用户、按 agent、按消耗、按用量、按交易结果、按业务结果)，说“没有哪一种能装下大多数客户”，所以给销售最大灵活性。这是回避。对投资者的含义：定价模式碎片化会让收入的可预测性下降，也让外部很难拆分 AI 收入。“降低流失率也是价值”说明在防守上 AI 的作用可能比进攻更实际。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用
So especially as we kind of cross now into the $50 billion in revenue, which very few software companies have ever done, we have a brand new product line and we have a brand new approach to pricing. So you saw in the second quarter, attrition had reached kind of a very low level, contract length had come to a very high level, cash flow and margin and revenue levels were very respectable and we had very clear trajectory going forward where you saw the cRPO growth, all of those things are critical and that I think our relevance has never been higher for these companies. Our ability to walk into any customer now and to show them a piece of technology that can radically transform their company very, very rapidly is unprecedented.
尤其是现在我们正在跨入 $50B 收入，很少有软件公司做到过，我们有了一条全新的产品线和全新的定价方式。你们看到在第二季度，流失率降到了很低的水平，合同期限达到了很高的水平，现金流、利润率和收入水平都很可观，未来轨迹也很清楚，你们看到了 cRPO 的增长，这些都很关键。我认为我们对这些公司的重要性从未这么高。我们现在能走进任何客户，给他们看一项能非常快速地彻底改变他们公司的技术，这是前所未有的。
解读几个需要校准的说法：(1) “跨入 $50B 收入”，按我们的理解 FY27 收入约 $46B，$50B 可能是指含近期收购的年化运行率，或是修辞，不应当作指引。(2) Q2 流失率“很低”、合同期限“很高”都没给数字。合同变长会抬高 cRPO (未来 12 个月要确认的合同收入)，所以 cRPO 增速里有一部分是合同期限变化的效果，不全是需求加速。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用

解读“不需要大量人力”“我不必雇几千人”：这对客户是卖点，对 Salesforce 的坐席收费是隐患。Benioff 自己讲的就是 AI 让企业用更少的人完成同样的事，如果客户也这样做，按人头收费的 Sales Cloud、Service Cloud 会承压。这是本场坐席风险最直白的表述之一，管理层没有把它和收入模型连起来讲。
Miguel Milano go-to-market 总负责人 (原 CRO，主持人，按上下文推断) 必读
So Robin, in preparing for this event last year, you introduced a new mantra that we are using in every single conversation, which is we have to meet customers wherever they are in the journey. (Miguel) Robin，去年筹备这个活动时，你提出了一句我们在每次对话里都用的口号：我们必须在客户旅程的任何阶段满足他们。那时我们有三四种变现方式和定价商业框架。之后我们加了一堆，Alexa 和我每次和客户谈，都会找到一种新的给产品定价的方式。Marc，你会高兴也会骄傲，你的信息非常清楚：我们现在非常激进地推基于结果的定价。Alexa 和我每个季度见几百个客户。今天我见了两个客户，提了两个基于结果的方案。一个是私募股权 (PE) 公司。我和这家 PE 公司约定：给我一家你们要投资、五年后卖掉的投资组合公司，我们投入大批人员和技术，按你们创造的价值收取一个百分比。到那时，比如他们 $2B 买进、$5B 卖出，假设我们的分成是 $300M，我们拿到 $300M 或 $400M。然后我们把它在五年里变成一份订阅合同。我们有很多方式。
解读定价，必读。Miguel 说正在“非常激进地”推基于结果的定价 (outcome-based pricing，按客户赚到或省下的钱分成)。举例：和一家 PE 公司谈，按被投公司增值的百分比收费，$2B 买、$5B 卖，Salesforce 拿 $300M 到 $400M，再摊成五年订阅。我们的看法：(1) 这类交易目前看是个案，规模和收入确认方式都没说；(2) 结果计费把 Salesforce 的收入和客户的经营结果、甚至 PE 退出价格绑在一起，波动性远高于坐席订阅；(3) 这也间接说明，管理层自己知道按坐席收费在 AI 时代撑不住增长，正在找替代。
Miguel Milano go-to-market 总负责人 (原 CRO，主持人，按上下文推断) 必读

问题 4：Marc Benioff、Karl (身份不详) CEO、提问者 (转写稿未列出，推断为分析师)

Marc Benioff、Karl (身份不详) CEO、提问者 (转写稿未列出，推断为分析师) 可跳过 (Benioff) 好，我们请后面的 Karl。好，太好了，谢谢。(提问内容在转写稿里被省略，02:52:02 到 02
Okay, let's go to Karl (02:51:56) back here, Okay, great. Thanks. (02:52:02-02:52:55). ...
(Benioff) 好，我们请后面的 Karl。好，太好了，谢谢。(提问内容在转写稿里被省略，02:52:02 到 02:52:55 约 1 分钟)
解读提问没有被转写，从回答内容推断，问题大概是 Salesforce 想把整个软件行业带向哪里、和其他企业应用厂商 (如 SAP (SAP)) 相比的位置。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 必读
I'm trying to take the whole industry somewhere. We try to do that. Obviously, we want to do that with our philanthropic model. So, let's take that off the table, the 1-1-1 model. We're trying to do that with our business model, but we are trying to do that with our technology model, too. And now we've slightly pivoted our technology model. So, I kind of went through the four layers with you exactly like as I've done it now with hundreds of customers on this European trip and then that kind of got to the point in the keynote. Now, we get into two different places. One is, I think a lot of you, but I don't know how many, not all of the enterprise applications companies, number one, are API first the way we are; and number two are not metadata first. There are still quite a few of these software companies that coded their applications. That is, there is still kind of a ghost of client-server past where the applications are fixed. They have not – they don't resolve into full metadata. Some of them are full metadata systems, but some of them are not.
……我在努力把整个行业带到某个地方。我们一直在这么做。显然我们想用我们的慈善模式做到这一点，先把 1-1-1 模式 (把 1% 的股权、产品和员工时间捐给公益) 放一边。我们想用我们的商业模式做到，也想用我们的技术模式做到。现在我们的技术模式稍微转了个方向。我和你们讲了这四层，就像我在这次欧洲之行中和几百个客户讲的一样，最后讲到了主题演讲里。现在我们分成两种情况。一是，我想你们很多人知道 (不知道有多少)，并不是所有企业应用公司第一都像我们一样是 API 优先 (API first)，第二都是元数据优先 (metadata first)。还有不少软件公司是把应用写死在代码里的，也就是说，还有一点客户端/服务器时代的“幽灵”：应用是固定的，不能分解成完整的元数据。有些是完整的元数据系统，但有些不是。
解读Benioff 在区分自己和其他企业应用厂商：Salesforce 是“API 优先、元数据优先”，应用可以被 AI 读懂并重组；有些竞争对手的应用是写死在代码里的。这是竞争定位的说法 (level 3 规则：对竞争对手的说法)，含义是 Salesforce 认为在 AI 界面时代自己比 SAP (SAP) 这类老架构更容易被 AI 调用。这个优势是真是假，要看 AI 时代客户是否还为“被调用”的系统付高价。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用
So, we have to kind of then bifurcate into those two worlds. For the ones who do have metadata systems that are API first, you are going to be able to move very, very rapidly. For the ones that aren't, it's going to look a little bit like, did you see the keynote where we brought up the SAP screens and we're scraping and then we're working with the agent in the screens. That's a good example of what that's going to look like for companies that have kind of more of that fixed application set, okay? A customer – all companies are going to really have to move to what our architecture is going to have to what it is.
所以我们要把它分成两个世界。对那些有元数据系统、API 优先的公司，你们能非常非常快地推进。对那些不是的公司，情况会有点像，你们看到主题演讲里我们调出 SAP (SAP) 的界面，然后我们在抓取屏幕 (screen scraping)，再让 agent 在这些界面里工作吗？对那些应用比较固定的公司，那就是会出现的样子。所有公司最终都必须转向我们这样的架构。
解读Benioff 公开点名 SAP (SAP)：在主题演讲里演示了 agent 抓取 SAP 屏幕来操作，暗示 SAP 的架构落后。我们的看法：这是竞争性喊话；SAP 在后台 (财务、供应链) 的地位和 Salesforce 在前台一样牢固，屏幕抓取的演示更多是营销。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用
The third piece is that when we're actually building these applications to help you run your business, we believe that the kind of things that the majority of employees in the company want to use, that is things that are focused on the customers, the products, the competitiveness of the company, its market position, it's ability to collaborate, to share, to market itself. Those applications are where we command and control the market. So, that is where we have a very unique and special position and that our applications are extremely relevant for our customers. In some cases, some of our competitors maybe have niche types of functionality. I don't have to get into all of the details, but some of their applications are not mainstream business data that is appropriate for large groups of users. Ours is because ours is for large groups of employees. That's what we're going to offer the most value in this application transformation. And I think that it kind of plays out with the customers. So, I think you'll see it kind of go forward.
第三点是，当我们真正构建这些帮你运营业务的应用时，我们认为公司里大多数员工想用的东西，也就是围绕客户、产品、公司竞争力、市场地位、协作、分享、营销能力的应用，这些是我们主导和掌控市场的地方。所以我们在这里有非常独特和特殊的位置，我们的应用对客户极其重要。在某些情况下，我们的一些竞争对手可能有小众功能，我不用细讲，但他们的一些应用不是适合大量用户的主流业务数据。我们的是，因为我们的应用是给大量员工用的。在这场应用变革中，我们会提供最大的价值。我认为在客户那里也是这样体现的。所以你们会看到它向前发展。
解读Benioff 的论点：Salesforce 的数据和应用是“大量员工都要用的主流业务数据”，竞争对手的是“小众功能”。这句话对坐席模型有两面：好的一面是用户面广；风险是如果 AI 让“大量员工”都通过 Claude 等界面使用 Salesforce 数据，而不是登录 Salesforce，坐席收费的基础会动摇。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 必读
Obviously, we've seen a couple killer apps so far in AI. One is the ChatGPT moment, okay? The second is the Claude Code moment where we have the coding agents and now we have the third one, which is kind of the Cowork area. And Anthropic's done a really good idea. That's why we're so proud to be like investors in Anthropic, like we – John did a brilliant maneuver by buying hundreds of millions of dollars of Anthropic stock that is turns into probably what will be tens of billions of dollars of Anthropic stock. Obviously, we probably will not hold that stock in the long term. That's not our role. We'll end up probably selling and paying off our ASR debt, so it'll be a good trade for us. We'll be trading our Anthropic stock for the 14% dilution that we gained back on our equity. Not bad trade. I'll take it any day of the week. So, thank you, John, for your leadership and great execution.
显然，到目前为止我们已经在 AI 里看到了几个杀手级应用。一个是 ChatGPT 时刻。第二个是 Claude Code 时刻，我们有了编程 agent。现在我们有第三个，就是 Cowork 这一块。Anthropic 做了一件很好的事。这也是为什么我们为成为 Anthropic 的投资者而骄傲。John (推断为 Salesforce Ventures 负责人) 做了一次很漂亮的操作，买入了数亿美元的 Anthropic 股票，现在可能会变成数百亿美元的 Anthropic 股票。显然我们大概不会长期持有这些股票，那不是我们的角色。我们最后可能会卖掉，用来偿还我们的 ASR 债务，所以这对我们是一笔很好的交易。我们会用 Anthropic 股票换回我们在股权上收回的 14% 稀释。不错的交易，我每天都愿意做。所以谢谢 John 的领导和出色执行。
解读本场问答里最有财务含义的一段，必读。(1) Salesforce (通过 Salesforce Ventures) 持有的 Anthropic 股权，成本“数亿美元”，Benioff 说可能值“数百亿美元” (tens of billions)，没给准确数。(2) 他首次明说：ASR (约减少 14% 股本的那笔加速回购) 部分靠借债完成，计划卖出 Anthropic 股票来还债。(3) 含义：如果 Anthropic 股权确实值 $20B 以上，相当于用投资收益为回购买单，资产负债表比看上去健康，这是我们 Deep 估值里没有单独计入的一项。但 Anthropic 是私有公司，估值和流动性都有不确定性，卖出时间也没说。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用
But we, I think, are extremely well positioned. And I do think that, yes, we will motivate a radical transformation for the industry. We'll get there faster because we command and control the market because of the size and scale of our distribution organization and because we cut (02:57:07) across small and medium businesses, large and very large businesses as well, and we can get to the mark – we are getting to the market faster. You can see that. I think that you'll probably say that maybe we're the first to show you what you saw yesterday. I think that that's extremely important. 但我认为我们处在极好的位置。我确实认为，是的，我们会推动行业的彻底变革。我们会更快到达那里，因为我们主导和掌控着市场，因为我们的销售组织规模庞大，因为我们覆盖中小企业、大企业和超大企业，我们能更快地进入市场。你们能看到这一点。我想你们大概会说，我们可能是第一个向你们展示昨天那些东西的。我认为这极其重要。你们非常密切地关注市场，我保证到年底，我们会在界面层再发布几项极其关键、令人振奋的公告。你们可以想象，我相信你们现在自己就能推演出界面层的各种机会。既然有了 AIforce，我们可以把其他每家供应商都接进来。显然我们必须选择优先和哪家供应商合作，我们做了选择，我认为我们做了正确的决定：第一的 AI 配第一的 CRM，这是我们的目标。但我们没有排他协议。我们会根据客户的“信仰”提供一整套界面。说得通吗？好。
解读两点：(1) 和 Anthropic 不是排他合作，年底前还会在界面层宣布更多合作 (按我们的理解可能包括 OpenAI、Google (GOOGL)、Microsoft (MSFT) 等)；(2) “第一的 AI 配第一的 CRM”是把 Anthropic 当首选。界面越多，Salesforce 越像一个被各家 AI 调用的数据和流程后台，这和本场 Headless 叙事一致，但都没有讲怎么按调用收费。

问题 5：Samik Chatterjee 分析师 (JPMorgan)

解读问 Salesforce 自研的 CRM 领域模型 Koa 的价值和路线。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 有用
Okay. And I'll have Rohan speak and I don't know if Silvio here also or not, so I'll have Rohan then kind of fill in the details. But I think for a while, we've been – we have – you probably know we have a long history of delivering models, about a decade. If you go to Hugging Face, you'll see so many of our models have become very pioneering, including prompt engineering itself was built at Salesforce, but models like BLIP and xGen and others. So, we've always had a vision, though, of actually delivering a CRM model. And we think that there are certain things that we could do for customers with the CRM model that would be quite good, but we have not really wanted the price tag, okay? But with Nemotron, we now kind of have the ability to fine-tune a model without spending an exorbitant cost. And so, that's the vision for Koa, is to take this vision that we have, which we call kind of CRM verse or customer verse and apply it to the Koa model and to use Nemotron as the reference architecture. And I think it'll be very exciting. Do you want to fill in the details?
好，我会请 Rohan 讲，不知道 Silvio (Salesforce 首席科学家 Silvio Savarese，推断) 在不在，我请 Rohan 补充细节。但一段时间以来，你们可能知道，我们有大约十年交付模型的历史。你去 Hugging Face 上看，会看到我们很多模型都很有开创性，包括提示词工程 (prompt engineering) 本身就是在 Salesforce 发明的，还有 BLIP、xGen 等模型。所以我们一直有一个愿景：交付一个 CRM 模型。我们认为用 CRM 模型能为客户做一些相当不错的事，但我们一直不想承担那个价格。但有了 Nemotron (NVIDIA (NVDA) 的开放权重模型)，我们现在有能力在不花巨额成本的情况下微调一个模型。这就是 Koa 的愿景：把我们称为 CRM verse 或 customer verse 的愿景应用到 Koa 模型上，并以 Nemotron 作为参考架构。我认为会很令人兴奋。你要补充细节吗？
解读Koa 是在 NVIDIA (NVDA) 开放权重模型 Nemotron 上做后训练 (微调) 的 CRM 专用模型。关键是“不想承担那个价格”：Salesforce 不会自己从头训练大模型，只做便宜的微调。含义是 Salesforce 的 AI 研发投入仍然克制，主要大脑还是外部模型 (Anthropic 等)，Koa 的作用可能是降低部分推理成本或满足特定客户，收入贡献没提。
Rohan Kumar Salesforce 数据与平台负责人 (推断) 有用
Yeah. No, I think, Marc, you captured it really well. So, there's maybe just one more thing that I'll add. Obviously, this 27 years of product making in CRM and sort of having those domain-specific models makes a lot of sense. And to Marc's point, like these open-weight models like Nemotron sort of make the post-training part of it a lot more amenable from a compute costs. That's what we've done. The other part essentially is this whole notion of data prep. So, you can imagine, like in the future, customers might want to use their own data in their own environment to sort of extend these open-weight models as well. So, there's an opportunity for us to actually build out that factory. Like, how do you go create that as a part of the data layer and make that available to the customers like if they – the most – the bigger enterprises want to go, build that on and they can do it themselves.
是的，Marc，你概括得很好。我只补充一点。显然，27 年在 CRM 领域做产品，拥有这些领域专用模型很合理。正如 Marc 说的，像 Nemotron 这样的开放权重模型让后训练在算力成本上更可行，这就是我们做的。另一部分是数据准备这件事。你可以想象，将来客户可能想在自己的环境里用自己的数据来扩展这些开放权重模型。所以我们有机会真正搭建那个“工厂”：怎么把它作为数据层的一部分构建出来、提供给客户，如果更大的企业想在上面自己构建，他们可以自己做。
解读Rohan Kumar 补充：未来可能让大客户用自己的数据在 Salesforce 数据层上微调开放权重模型，Salesforce 提供“模型工厂”。这是 Data Cloud 的潜在新收入来源，但目前只是设想。
Marc Benioff 董事长兼 CEO (问答唯一主答人，推断) 可跳过 (Benioff) 这只针对非常有限的一部分客户。就像我说的，我认为大多数客户会想通过应用本身、通过打包软件获得这种能力

(主持人) 你后面是 Adam，请把话筒递给他。(Adam) 嗨，谢谢。我是 Morgan Stanley 的 Adam Wood。Marc，你讲了很多技术变革和软件交付方式的变化。我们也看到在付费方式上有很多创新，你也暗示了它现在怎么在变。当我们思考用什么替代坐席作为软件的计价单位时，你在中期有没有一个愿景，最可能替代坐席的模式是什么？其次，如果今天很难判断，你对今年下半年你们看到的有机收入再加速有多大信心能平稳持续，而不是因为不同定价模式的扩散而变得起伏不定？谢谢。
解读本场最尖锐的两个问题：(1) 什么会替代坐席成为计价单位？(2) H2 有机再加速能否平稳持续，会不会因为定价模式五花八门而波动？两个都直接关系到我们的核心疑问。
